# zygo phase 2: workspaces — design

**Status:** approved by the operator 2026-11-21
**Date:** 2026-11-20
**Release:** phase 2
**Base spec:** `docs/specs/2026-11-18-zygo-design.md`.

**Citation rule.** A bare `§n` is a section of the base spec. `W§n` is a section of this document. This document changes the base spec only where W§15 says so.

Each decision has a mark. **(operator)**: the operator made the decision. **(author)**: the author of this document made the decision; the operator can change it before approval. Code references are `file:line` at commit `48f9583`.

---

## 1. Intent

The operator runs several agents in tmux. The operator must stop them for two reasons:

1. **Reason 1:** to free resources, so that the operator can use the workstation for other work.
2. **Reason 2:** to restart the coding harness after an upgrade (for example, a new Claude Code version).

Today zygo cannot pause or resume. A zygo session is one launch of one agent (§5). At exit the launcher deletes `~/.cache/zygo/sessions/<ULID>/` except `results/` (`crates/zygo-launch/src/launcher.rs:729-754`, §8.2 step 6). zygo does not record the agent's own session id. Thus, after a stop, the operator must find each conversation again by hand and rebuild the tmux layout by hand.

With elephant-goldfish (§9), a lead agent is usually idle at its prompt while its subagents work. The operator, or an agent that the operator tells to, runs `zygo down`.

Phase 2 adds **workspaces**. `zygo down` waits until each agent of one repository has ended its turn and its subagents (the drain), then saves the state and stops the agents. `zygo up` brings back the tmux layout and resumes each agent in its own conversation, with its saved launch flags, its session overrides and its compaction override (OP12).

## 2. Goals

- **W-G1** `zygo up` creates or restores one tmux session per repository, with its layout and its agents.
- **W-G2** `zygo down` drains, then saves and stops all agents of the workspace.
- **W-G3** `zygo up` after `zygo down`, after a reboot, or after a crash of the tmux server resumes each agent in its own conversation.
- **W-G4** A loadout in user config sets the first layout and the agent panes of a workspace.
- **W-G5** CLI verbs list, attach, and forget workspaces.
- **W-G6** No daemon. tmux is the only long-lived process.

## 3. Non-goals

These items are out of scope for phase 2 **(operator)**. Reason: the scope is reduced. Elephant-goldfish subagents make a cross-agent coordination layer unnecessary for now. Each item can return in a later phase.

- A supervisor daemon.
- Messages between agents, and wake of one agent by another.
- Triggers (actions that start on an event).
- Git modes (four modes in an earlier draft) and automatic worktree management. zygo does not create, delete, or switch worktrees.
- Pools and slots of agents.
- Toasts (desktop notifications).
- Restart briefs (text that zygo writes for a fresh restart).
- Separate `pause` and `resume` verbs. `down` and `up` do this work **(operator, OP1)**.
- Workspaces on native Windows **(operator, OP5)**.
- Restore of non-agent commands. zygo never runs a saved shell command again **(operator, OP8)**.
- A change to the current work item. Today the explicit setting is shared in the git common dir (`crates/zygo-work/src/current.rs:66-67`), and detection reads the branch of the checkout that holds the cwd (`current.rs:102-107`), so detection can already differ per worktree. Phase 2 keeps both. A per-worktree setting is related work for a later phase.
- Changes to the usage store schema (§14).

## 4. Constraints

- **W-C1** The base constraints C1-C10 (§4) apply. C6 applies to every new hook: a hook never breaks the agent.
- **W-C2** No daemon (§7.1, A2, §18.3). tmux holds the processes; zygo runs only when a verb, a hook, or a pane starts it.
- **W-C3** zygo sets tmux options and hooks only on its own sessions. zygo never sets a global tmux option or a global hook.
- **W-C4** Every state write is atomic: write a temporary file, then rename it.
- **W-C5** A cloned repository cannot start an agent through a workspace until the operator approves its workspace files on this machine (workspace definition design R§7). Loadouts come from user config, and from `.zygo/workspace.toml` after that approval. A repository loadout never starts a shell pane and never sets agent arguments (workspace definition design R-C3).
- **W-C6** `zygo claude` and `zygo opencode` outside a workspace stay a clean v1 launch **(operator, OP9)**.
- **W-C7** The workspace state holds no secret and no content: no session token, no provider key, no URL user info, no prompt text, no conversation content, no subagent description, and no inline value (JSON text or URL) of an agent flag (AU30). W§7.2 lists every field.
- **W-C8** No user-controlled text reaches a tmux argument that tmux expands as a format, unescaped (AU55, AU54).
- **W-C9** Never resume a conversation whose launcher is live, and never let two pane keys hold one agent session: a hand start whose own session argument is the `agent_session` of a record under another pane key, or its `pending.agent_session` while the launcher of that pending start is live (the live rule of `Store::live_launcher` on `pending.zygo_session`), of any agent kind, in any state except `ended`, is refused with `zygo: agent session <id> belongs to pane <other key>; resume it in that pane, or run zygo ws forget-pane <other key>` (exit 1, nothing started). The start write of a hand start with an own session argument and no pre-assigned id sets that argument as `pending.agent_session` (W§8.4), so a second hand start with the same argument is refused also before a hook commits the first one; a pending id whose launcher is not live (a start that died) holds nothing (operator, AU87, AU94).

## 5. Glossary additions

§5 "Session: one launch of one agent" does not change. A resumed agent is a new zygo session with the same agent conversation.

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Workspace | One git repository, identified by its git common dir. The main checkout and all linked worktrees are one workspace **(operator, OP2)**. |
| Workspace session | The tmux session of a workspace, named `zygo-<name>`. One per workspace **(operator, OP3)**. |
| Workspace id | The first 16 hex characters of SHA-256 of the canonical common-dir path. Form: `^[0-9a-f]{16}$` (AU2, AU84). |
| Pane key | A stable id that zygo gives each pane of a workspace session: `k` plus 8 random characters of lower-case base32. Form: `^k[a-z2-7]{8}$` (AU84). It stays the same after a restore. tmux pane ids (`%N`) change. |
| Member | The agent slot of one pane key. A member runs one zygo agent session at a time; its record (`member` in W§7.2) persists across zygo sessions, so the next `up` can resume it. |
| Agent session id | The agent's own conversation id (Claude Code `session_id`, OpenCode `sessionID`). zygo uses it to resume. Not the zygo session id (ULID). |
| Live launcher | A launcher whose session directory passes `session_dir::live` (`crates/zygo-core/src/session_dir.rs:13-17`): it holds the lock on its `pid` file. |
| Loadout | A named layout plus its panes, in user config **(operator, OP10)**. "Profile" stays the model profile (§11). |
| Loadout pane | One `[[loadout.<name>.window.pane]]` entry (W§11): an agent pane or a shell pane. |
| Snapshot | The saved state of a workspace: layout, panes, cwds, and member records. |
| Drain | The wait in `zygo down` until each member's turn and its subagents end. |
| Detached member | A member record whose pane key is not in the saved layout. `up` does not recreate it (W§7.5). |
| Builder | The person or agent who implements this design. |
| Brief | The task text that started this document. W§20 lists where the repository contradicts it. |

## 6. Decided approach

### 6.1 Parts

```
 operator, or an agent the operator tells to
        │  zygo up | down | ls | attach | loadout | ws forget
        ▼
 ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
 │ zygo CLI (crates/zygo/src/cmd)                                   │
 │   └─ crates/zygo-workspace: identity, state store, lock,         │
 │      snapshot, loadout resolution, drain, Multiplexer trait      │
 │      └─ Tmux impl ── tmux -S <absolute socket path> ──┐          │
 └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────────┘
                                                         ▼
 ┌────────────────────── tmux server ───────────────────────────────┐
 │ session zygo-<name>   env ZYGO_WORKSPACE=<id>   option @zygo-ws  │
 │  hooks (this session only) ── run-shell ──▶ zygo ws snapshot     │
 │  ┌─ pane @zygo-pane=k1 ───────────┐  ┌─ pane @zygo-pane=k2 ─────┐ │
 │  │ zygo ws run (argv, no shell)   │  │ zygo ws run --shell      │ │
 │  │  └─ launcher (§8.2) + proxy    │  │  └─ exec $SHELL in cwd   │ │
 │  │      └─ claude --resume <id>   │  └──────────────────────────┘ │
 │  │          hooks: SessionStart,  │                               │
 │  │          UserPromptSubmit,     │──▶ zygo hook workspace        │
 │  │          PreToolUse Agent,     │                               │
 │  │          Stop, StopFailure,    │                               │
 │  │          SubagentStart/Stop    │                               │
 │  │  then exec $SHELL              │                               │
 │  └────────────────────────────────┘                               │
 └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                                      │ atomic writes under lock
                                                      ▼
 ~/.local/state/zygo/workspaces/<id>/workspace.json   (snapshot, members)
 ~/.cache/zygo/sessions/<ULID>/activity.json           (turn and subagent state)
 ~/.cache/zygo/sessions/<ULID>/results ──symlink──▶ ~/.cache/zygo/workspaces/<id>/results/<pane key>/
```

### 6.2 Decisions

A withdrawn call keeps its id so that earlier reviews stay traceable.

| Id | Decision | Mark |
|---|---|---|
| OP1 | Scope: workspace, loadout, one tmux session per workspace, `zygo down`, `zygo up`, CLI verbs to manage sessions. No pause or resume verbs. | operator |
| OP2 | A workspace is one repository, identified by the git common dir. The main checkout and every linked worktree are the same workspace. Source: `Checkout { top, common_dir, main_root }`, `crates/zygo-work/src/repo.rs:17-22`, found by `find_checkout`, `repo.rs:119-135`. | operator |
| OP3 | One tmux session per workspace. | operator |
| OP4 | `zygo up` resumes each agent's own conversation by its saved agent session id. This reverses the earlier draft's "fresh restart by default". | operator |
| OP5 | Native Windows has no workspace features. Each workspace verb exits 2 with `workspaces need tmux; run zygo in WSL`. `zygo claude` works as in v1. The multiplexer is behind a trait; tmux is the only phase-2 implementation. | operator |
| OP6 | `down` drains by default. zygo blocks new subagent dispatch during the drain. `[workspace] drain_block_dispatch = true` is the default. | operator |
| OP7 | zygo snapshots the full state, layout included, on every change while the workspace runs, not only at `down`. | operator |
| OP8 | A pane that ran a zygo agent session is resumed. Every other pane comes back as a plain shell in its saved cwd. zygo never runs a saved non-agent command again. | operator |
| OP9 | `zygo claude` and `zygo opencode` outside a workspace stay a clean v1 launch. Only `zygo up` creates or restores a workspace. | operator |
| OP10 | The name is "loadout". "Profile" stays the model profile (`crates/zygo-config/src/profile.rs`). A loadout pane can name a model profile. | operator |
| OP11 | The workspace verbs `down`, `ls` and the new `workspace` (status of this workspace) are also `/zygo:` slash commands, per O14: `/zygo:down [--now]`, `/zygo:ls`, `/zygo:workspace`. `up` and `attach` stay CLI-only: they act on a terminal, and an `up` that an agent runs acts as with `--no-attach` (AU95). `/zygo:down` runs with `ZYGO_SESSION` set, so it takes the detached path (AU18) and replies within the hook bound. Replaces AU21. | operator |
| OP12 | Session overrides (`overrides.json`) and the compaction override of a member are persisted. They are saved on each change, not at `down`: each code path that changes one also writes the new value into the member record when the session is a member (AU105). At resume the saved values are always applied, with no `clean_stop` gate. A crash or reboot does not lose them. Replaces AU101. | operator |
| OP13 | AU91 is approved: the proxy session status gains `requests_in_flight` and `last_request_end`, on team servers too. | operator |
| AU1 | No daemon. Each agent pane runs a normal launcher. tmux hooks on the workspace session run `zygo ws snapshot`. Launchers and agent hooks write member records. This keeps §7.1, A2 and §18.3. It amends A2 "session state dies with the session" only: a workspace state directory outlives sessions. | author |
| AU2 | State directory `~/.local/state/zygo/workspaces/<id>/` (dir `0700`, files `0600`). `id` = the first 16 hex characters of SHA-256 of the canonical common-dir path (`Checkout.common_dir`; the `canon` closure at `repo.rs:126` makes it canonical at `:129`). Name = slug of the main worktree's directory name (AU77), fixed at creation. Slug rule: lower-case the name; change each character outside `[a-z0-9-]` to `-`; collapse each run of `-` to one; trim `-` at both ends; cut to 32 characters and trim a trailing `-` again; an empty result is `ws`. On collision zygo adds `-2`, `-3`, … (the slug part is cut so that the whole stays at 32 characters or less). tmux session `zygo-<name>`. | author |
| AU3 | Agent session id: the `SessionStart` hook records `session_id` from the main loop each time it fires (it fires again after `/clear` with a new id; PW2), directly in `member.agent_session`. For Claude Code, zygo also pre-assigns `--session-id=<uuid>` when the arguments hold no session argument (W§8.3). Resume: Claude Code `--resume=<id>`, OpenCode `--session=<id>`, each one token, so an id that starts with `-` is never read as another flag. Claude Code takes the `=` form, which its pinned help shows for options of the same parser. OpenCode 1.19.6 binds `--session=<value>` to its session option: `opencode run --session=-x` reaches the session id check with `-x`, while `opencode run -s -x` is a usage error (probe of 2026-11-22); PW4 verified `opencode run -s <id>`, the same option. The caller passes the id as `LaunchOpts.agent_session` (`Preassign(id)` or `Resume(id)`); the launcher inserts the adapter's `preassign_args` or `resume_args` before the user's arguments, so before any `--`, and for `opencode run` right after `run`. | author |
| AU4 | Drain detection from member hooks and the proxy (W§9.1, AU26). Dispatch block: `PreToolUse` deny on the subagent tool (W§9.2). Default timeout `[workspace] drain_timeout = "10m"`. On timeout `down` stops nothing, reports what still runs, and suggests `--now`. See W§9. | author |
| AU5 | `zygo down --now` stops at once and records the subagents that were running (id and type only). At the next `up` that agent gets one note at session start (W§9.4). | author |
| AU6 | `zygo down` detaches by the rule of AU18: it starts the drain in a detached process and returns at once. Otherwise `down` runs in the foreground with progress. `zygo ls` shows `draining`. | author |
| AU7 | `zygo up` outside tmux creates the session (if needed) and attaches. Inside the user's own tmux on the workspace's server it creates the session detached and runs `switch-client`; on another server it prints the attach command (AU100). `--no-attach` skips both; `ZYGO_SESSION` set implies `--no-attach` (AU95). Minimum tmux version: 3.2 (PW6, 2026-11-22: verified with the first-window resize of AU96). | author |
| AU8 | An agent pane runs `zygo ws run` (AU15). When the agent exits by itself, the pane becomes a shell in its cwd, and the snapshot records it as a shell (OP8). | author |
| AU9 | A `zygo claude` or `zygo opencode` that the operator starts by hand in a pane of a workspace session joins the workspace. It is snapshotted and resumed like a loadout agent, and gets the same `--session-id` pre-assign (W§8.3). Detection: W§8.4. A pane key that holds a resumable record refuses the hand start (AU94). | author |
| AU10 | Loadouts live in user config, `[loadout.<name>]`, and, after an approval, in `.zygo/workspace.toml` as the loadout `repository` (workspace definition design R§9.1). `.zygo.toml` still cannot set `[loadout]` or `[workspace]`: it keeps its four tables (`crates/zygo-config/src/repo_file.rs:39`, filter at `:277-287`). Built-in loadout `solo`. First `up` applies the loadout; a later `up` restores the last snapshot; `up --reset` applies the loadout again. Schema: W§11. | author |
| AU11 | Per member zygo restores: agent kind, agent session id, saved launch flags and arguments (AU30), model profile, passthrough, server, config dir, session overrides and compaction override (OP12, AU32). The current work item stays as it is today. The resume note names the work units that the stop closed (AU31). | author |
| AU12 | A failed resume (W§8.2 step 7) opens a shell and prints the resume command. Every later `up` tries the resume again (AU60). The missing-cwd case: AU61. | author |
| AU13 | Serve mode: a resumed agent is a new zygo session with a new ULID and a new token. No usage-schema change. | author |
| AU14 | New crate `crates/zygo-workspace`: identity, state store, lock, snapshot, loadout resolution, membership, drain, `Multiplexer` trait and tmux implementation. CLI verbs in `crates/zygo/src/cmd/` (enum `Cmd` at `crates/zygo/src/cmd/mod.rs:131-241`, dispatch at `:394-418`). | author |
| AU15 | tmux starts every pane that `up` creates with an argument list, not a shell string: `<zygo> ws run --ws-id <id> --pane <key>` for an agent pane, `<zygo> ws run --ws-id <id> --pane <key> --shell` for a shell pane (AU46). `ws run` reads the member record, runs the launcher in process, and then replaces itself (`exec`) with `$SHELL` (else `/bin/sh`) in the pane's cwd. No shell quoting is needed. | author |
| AU16 | Result ids stay readable after a resume, with no move. The result tools read only the current session's `results/` (`crates/zygo-mcp/src/tools/results.rs:9-10`). The launcher callback (AU34) makes `~/.cache/zygo/workspaces/<id>/results/<pane key>/` and makes the new session's `results` a symlink to it, as an absolute path under `workspace_cache_dir`. Each of `<id>`, `results` and `<pane key>` is made when missing, opened without following a link relative to its parent's open folder, and set to mode `0700` through its own open folder, so a part that exists with another mode gets `0700` too. A symbolic link at `<id>`, `results` or `<pane key>` is not followed: the callback makes nothing and logs the error, and the launch goes on (the check of `ws forget-pane`, W§12). The result store creates or reuses the directory through the link (`Store::put`, `crates/zygo-results/src/store.rs:56-57`). At exit, `remove_except_results` keeps the `results` entry and its `remove_dir` of the session directory fails, because the link is still there (`crates/zygo-launch/src/session_dir.rs:86-100`). The base sweep keeps a session directory whose `results` is a symlink that is not under `workspace_cache_dir`. A session directory whose `results` link is under `workspace_cache_dir` is removed by AU69: by the base 24 h age rule, when it is not live and still not live just before the removal, the link first; the link target and the workspace results folder stay. | author |
| AU17 | The member wrapper classifies the agent's exit. If the launcher **received** SIGTERM or SIGHUP at any time during the launch (the `received` flag of AU34), or a signal ended the agent (the separate signal of AU73), the member stays `resumable`. A resume that failed fast (W§8.2 step 7) stays `resumable` too. Any other exit after a spawn sets `ended`, and the pane is a shell from then on. An exit without a spawn keeps the member as it was (AU60). `signals::stopped()` (`crates/zygo-launch/src/signals.rs:74-79`) cannot give this: it reads `PENDING`, which `handle` and `wait` reset when they forward a signal (`signals.rs:116`, `:91`), and it is `pub(crate)`. The launcher receives SIGTERM from `down` and forwards it (`handle`, `signals.rs:105-130`); on a tmux hangup or a system shutdown the agent, in the same process group (§8.2 step 5), receives the signal directly too. | author |
| AU18 | Detach rule: `down` detaches when `ZYGO_SESSION` is set and not empty (an agent's tool runs it, in any pane or outside tmux), or when `$TMUX_PANE` is a pane of a target workspace session. `--wait` forces the foreground. An empty `ZYGO_SESSION` is no session id: `zygo down` then does not detach for it, and `/zygo:down` passes the id of its session directory instead (the `session` of its `view.json`; the directory name when that `session` is empty or `view.json` does not load), so `/zygo:down` runs detached when that id is not empty or when `$TMUX_PANE` is a pane of the target workspace session, and in the foreground otherwise. Reasons: in its own workspace, `down` would wait for its caller's turn and then kill its own pane; in any agent, the Bash tool has its own timeout, which a 10 min wait passes. On Linux, before it detaches, `down` compares its mount and pid namespaces with the tmux server's (`/proc/self/ns/mnt` and `/proc/self/ns/pid` with `/proc/<pid>/ns/mnt` and `/proc/<pid>/ns/pid`) and checks that `/proc/<pid>/comm` starts with `tmux`. When one of them cannot be read or differs (an agent sandbox, PW15), it does not detach and exits 1 with `zygo: down cannot run detached from a sandboxed shell; use the workspace_down tool ([features] workspace_tool) or /zygo:down`. The guard runs for every target before any session check: for each server it first checks a saved socket (AU62), then reads the server pid as the first line of `list-sessions -F '#{pid}'`, so a target whose session is gone does not decide it. An answer that is not a number counts as unreadable (the line above). A server that cannot be reached (any tmux error, also no server) does not detach and exits 1 with `zygo: cannot reach the tmux server of <name> (<tmux error>); in a sandboxed shell, run zygo down outside it; if that server is gone, run zygo down --wait -- <name> from a shell outside the workspace`. With `--all`, it runs once per server; each target on a server that cannot be reached gets that line and is left out, the other targets go on, and the exit code is 1. On other systems than Linux it passes. | author |
| AU19 | `zygo down --all` drains every workspace whose tmux session exists at the same time, under one timeout. Each workspace enters `stopping` and stops as soon as it drains, without a wait for the others (M12). A workspace with `phase = up`, no tmux session and no live launcher gets `phase = down` and `down_all = true`. A workspace whose `op.lock` is held, or whose state does not parse, is skipped and reported (AU80); the exit code is then 1. | author |
| AU20 | `zygo up <name>` restores a workspace by name from any directory. Without a name, `up` uses the workspace of the cwd. | author |
| AU21 | Withdrawn by the operator; OP11 replaces it. | author |
| AU22 | Only the built-in layer and the user layer can set `[loadout]` and `[workspace]`. zygo drops them from the team layer (§6.1 layer 2) with one warning line. Reason: a loadout starts programs with arguments, and a team server must not start programs on a client. The repository loadout is not a layer; it is added after an approval (workspace definition design R§9.1). A model profile (§6.1 layer 5) needs no change: `apply_profile` merges only the profile's `features` table (`crates/zygo-config/src/layers.rs:694-708`); a repository profile has none (workspace definition design R§8). | author |
| AU23 | Withdrawn; OP12 replaces it. | author |
| AU24 | At `down`, zygo lists shell panes whose foreground program is not a shell (`#{pane_current_command}`). A dead pane (`#{pane_dead}` is 1) is not listed: it runs no program, though tmux still names its last one. It warns about them and stops them with the session. It does not refuse. Reason: `down` can run with no person at the terminal (an agent runs it). | author |
| AU25 | `zygo up` outside a git repository exits 2 with `zygo up needs a git repository`. | author |
| AU26 | Idle rule (W§9.3 step 5). A member is idle when all three are true: (1) `busy` is `false`: a `Stop` or `StopFailure` came after the last `UserPromptSubmit`; (2) its running set (`SubagentStart`/`SubagentStop`) is empty; a `SubagentStop` with no matching `SubagentStart` (internal agents, empty `agent_type`; PW7 7.5) does not change the running set; (3) its proxy reports no model request in flight and none that ended in the last 10 s (AU91). A request in flight makes the member busy whatever `last_request_end` holds. A `last_request_end` that is 10 s or less after the poll time counts as a request that just ended. Rule 3 is skipped when the proxy status has no `requests_in_flight` field (an older team server), or when no request is in flight and `last_request_end` is not an RFC 3339 time or is more than 10 s after the poll time; `down` then prints this line once for the member: `zygo: <member>: proxy request state not usable; idle rule 3 skipped`. A proxy status that cannot be read is not a skip: the member is busy with the reason `proxy status not read: <error>`. A missing `activity.json` reads as `busy` `false` and an empty running set (W§9.1); an `activity.json` that cannot be read or parsed makes the member busy with the reason `activity file unreadable: <error>`. Running tool (W10 round 3, busy over idle): a member whose main loop has a running tool (`tools_running` above 0, W§9.1) is busy with the reason `tool running`, whatever `busy` holds and whatever time passes: a tool call sends no model request. Open turn (FIX-20, operator 2026-11-22: no invented requirement may end user work): a member whose `busy` is `true` and whose main loop runs no tool is busy at every poll, whatever time passes; no quiet time counts it as idle. Events off and hooks off (FIX-24, controller 2026-11-22): a member whose agent sends no member events is busy at every poll, whatever its `activity.json` and its proxy status show, until its agent exits (a member whose launcher is not live counts as idle, W§9.3 step 5), the drain timeout or `down --now`; no timeout, cap or clean-up is added, and the user's arguments and settings are kept. The checks, in this order: (a) the member record's `launch.events_off` is `true` (W§7.2: the start ran Claude Code with `--bare`, which skips the hooks of settings, `fixtures/claude-code/help.txt:40-44`, or with `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` on in the environment that its agent gets: the environment of the start after the main spec §8.4 deny list (`crates/zygo-launch/src/env.rs`, `child_env`), which the launcher gives to the member callback; zygo sets no `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` itself, and a provider key named `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` is removed by the deny list, so it does not count. The variable is on by Claude Code's own rule: its value, in lower case and trimmed, is one of `1`, `true`, `yes`, `on`; so `1`, `TRUE`, ` yes ` and `on` are on, and `false`, `no`, `off`, `2`, ` 0`, an empty value and an unset variable are off. Evidence: the installed Claude Code 2.1.331 binary (`~/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.331`) turns bare mode on with `De(process.env.CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE)||x7t("--bare")`, where `De` computes `String(e).toLowerCase().trim()` and returns whether `["1","true","yes","on"]` includes it; the pinned help says that `--bare` sets `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1`, `fixtures/claude-code/help.txt:47-48`. Such a start is also logged as degraded and refused with `--passthrough`, as a `--bare` start is (W§15 rows `§8.5 Flags` and `§12.3`); or OpenCode with `--pure`, the subcommand `attach`, or the subcommand `run` with `--attach`, the subcommand found by the rule of W§7.2 `member.launch`, AU27 known limit 8), with the reason `member events off: launched with --bare, --pure or attach`; (b) the member's session folder `settings.json` (the merged file that zygo wrote and that its Claude Code runs with, AU59) is a JSON object whose top-level `disableAllHooks` is `true` (main spec §8.5 Flags: a user `disableAllHooks: true` wins and stops every hook, zygo's included), with the reason `hooks off: its --settings sets disableAllHooks`. For such a member `down` still prints the rule-skipped line of rule 3 when its proxy status meets a skip case. `down` reads that file at each poll; the read never waits on a FIFO and, on unix, never follows a link; a missing file, a link, an entry that is not a regular file, a file that cannot be read, is not JSON or is not a JSON object, and a `disableAllHooks` that is not `true` do not count. This file is the only source to check: zygo's settings set `disableAllHooks: false`, and `--settings` outranks the user, project and local settings files (main spec §8.4), while the preflight stops a launch whose managed settings file sets `disableAllHooks: true` or `allowManagedHooksOnly: true` (main spec §8.4). The running set of `activity.json` is still read and printed in the timeout report. Known limit: zygo reads only the managed settings file; a `disableAllHooks: true` from a `managed-settings.d/` folder, an MDM or registry policy or server-managed settings (main spec §8.4: not checked) stops the member hooks, and the drain does not see it, so such a member can look idle during a long tool call. Known limit: a `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` that a settings file sets through its `env` key (`fixtures/claude-code/settings-schema.json:1192`) is not read. Known limit: a turn ended with Esc gives no `Stop` or `StopFailure` (PW7 7.2), so `busy` stays `true`. The member then stays busy until a `Stop` or `StopFailure` comes (a `Stop` comes at the end of the turn of a next prompt, if one comes; without one the turn stays open until the drain timeout or `down --now`), until its agent exits (a member whose launcher is not live counts as idle, W§9.3 step 5), or until the drain timeout, where `down` writes `phase = up` again with no work lost, stops nothing, lists the member with `no Stop since its last prompt` and exits 1; `down --now` is the operator's explicit override. Known limit: a turn that starts with no `UserPromptSubmit` (for example, when a background task ends) and runs a tool for more than 10 s without a model request can look idle. Known limit: an Esc during a tool fires no hook (P-N24-1, `docs/live-checks.md`: an interrupted command fires neither `PostToolUse` nor `PostToolUseFailure`), so `tools_running` stays above 0. The member then stays busy until its next `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop` or `SessionEnd`, each of which sets the count to 0; else `down` reaches its drain timeout, writes `phase = up` again with no work lost and exits 1, and `down --now` stops it. A lost `PostToolUse` (its hook did not get `activity.lock` within its bound, AU56), and a tool call that another `PreToolUse` hook denied after the count went up, do the same. On OpenCode a tool call with no `tool.execute.after` (AU27 known limit 6) does the same until the plugin's next `prompt` or `stop` call. Known limit: a hook that does not get `activity.lock` within its 1 s bound loses its change (AU56): a lost `SubagentStop` keeps the member busy until the drain timeout, and a lost `SubagentStart` hides that subagent from rule 2. An `activity.json` that cannot be read or parsed, or that is larger than 1 MiB, stays busy: no hook replaces it, and no hook writes a result larger than 1 MiB (AU56). Known limit: an `activity.json` that does not parse is never rewritten, so the member counts busy for the rest of the agent run: every graceful `down` waits the drain timeout and exits 1 until `down --now` or the agent exits (a session folder is never reused, so this ends with the agent). Known limit: with hostile id sizes a `Stop` or a `SubagentStart` can be refused (AU56): the member stays busy until the next accepted `Stop`, or a background subagent is not seen by rule 2. A `/zygo:` line that the prompt hook consumes as a verb never reaches the model, except when its verb panics (W§9.1 `UserPromptSubmit` row), so `workspace --event prompt` does not change `activity.json` for it (a verb line such as `/zygo:status` starts no turn); a line that the prompt hook passes to the model sets `busy = true`. Both hooks use the same rule (`prompt::consumes`). The `zygo` binary registers the dispatch before any hook runs, so every `/zygo:` line that `prompt::consumes` accepts is consumed; other lines (for example a leading space or an unknown form) pass to the model and mark the member busy. Known limit: when the `zygo hook prompt` process is killed from outside before it decides (for example by Claude Code's hook timeout), Claude Code sends the line to the model and that turn is not marked busy. Rule 3 (the proxy) and `tools_running` still apply to that turn. | author |
| AU27 | OpenCode turn signals and main session (W§9.1). The plugin keeps, per OpenCode process: the kind of each session, `top` or `child`, set only from the `info` of a `session.*` event (`info.parentID` present and not null gives `child`, whatever its value); the first value is final, since `parentID` is set when a session is created and does not change; a session with no `info` seen has no kind. An activation number per session from one counter: a `session.created` event activates `info.id`, and a `session.status` `busy` event activates its session; an event that only carries `info` (`session.updated` or any other type) classifies and does not activate. A `session.deleted` event classifies `info.id` as any `info` event does (a deleted child stays a child) and marks it deleted; from then on it is never the main session and is never sent. The main session is the known top session that is not deleted and has the highest activation; when it changes to a session other than the one sent last, the plugin sends `zygo hook workspace --event session` with that `sessionID` (AU104). When the main session is deleted, the main session becomes the known top session that is not deleted and has the highest activation, and the plugin sends it if it is not the one sent last; when there is no such session, the plugin has no main session and sends nothing. A child is never sent, in any event order. The turn set: a `busy` status of a session that is not a known child adds it; an idle event (`session.idle`, or `session.status` `idle`) removes it; its classification as a child removes it; its `session.deleted` removes it. When the set becomes non-empty the plugin sends `--event prompt` with the id that was added, which acts as `UserPromptSubmit` (`busy = true`); when it becomes empty, `--event stop` with the id that left, which acts as `Stop` (`busy = false`). A `retry` status and repeated signals send nothing. At load (the plugin factory, member gate on) the plugin sends `--event session` with no `sessionID`, which commits the pending start (AU92); the factory runs only in a spawned agent. A `session` call does not change `activity.json` (AU104): an open turn stays busy until its `stop`, the agent exit, the drain timeout or `down --now`. The plugin decides each call when the event arrives, before any wait, and runs the calls one after another. All three rules of AU26 apply to OpenCode members, and so do its open-turn rule and its known limits; rule 2 always holds, because OpenCode sends no subagent events (A-19). The tool count: for a session id that is not a known child (the population of the turn set), `tool.execute.before` of every tool other than `task` queues `--event pre-tool` with `{"sessionID": <id>, "tool_name": <the OpenCode tool name>, "cwd": <directory>}` before the guard runs and does not wait for it; `tool.execute.before` of `task`, in every session, queues `--event pre-tool` with `tool_name` `"Agent"`, waits for it, and a deny throws (W§9.1). A call is counted when its `pre-tool` is queued, before any wait (for `task`, before the wait for its answer); a deny answer to the `task` call forgets it, and a `prompt` or `stop` queued during that wait has already forgotten it; the plugin keeps it by its session id and its `callID` together (both non-empty strings; both hooks carry them, `fixtures/opencode/plugin-hooks/`). `tool.execute.after` of a counted call queues `--event tool-end` with that session id first, before `trim` and `work`, and forgets the call; it does not check the kind again, so a session classified as a child between the two hooks still sends its `tool-end`; any other `tool.execute.after` sends nothing. When the plugin sends `prompt` or `stop`, it forgets every counted call of every session, because the hook then sets the one member count to 0. Known limits: (1) a session that becomes active with no `info` event in this process (a TUI switch to an existing session, when OpenCode sends no `session.updated` for it) is never sent: `member.agent_session` keeps the earlier top session, so `up` resumes that one; its turn signals still work; E2 records whether `session.updated` fires on a TUI switch. (2) A deleted session is never sent after its `session.deleted` event. A record made before the delete is kept when no other known top session exists, and `up` then tries it (a resume of a deleted session fails and AU12 applies); otherwise the record moves to the newest known top session that is not deleted (the one with the highest activation). No fixture shows `session.deleted`: a session deleted with no `session.deleted` event in this process stays recorded until another top session becomes the main session. (3) A session in a turn whose idle event does not come keeps the member busy until its agent exits, the drain timeout or `down --now` (AU26). (4) A child whose `busy` comes before its `info` is in the turn set until its `info` or its idle. While its parent's turn is open, `busy` does not change. When the parent's idle comes while such a child is still in the set, the set stays non-empty: the member stays busy until that child's idle (which then sends `stop` with the child's id), its `info`, the agent exit, the drain timeout or `down --now`; both outcomes are the safe side. (5) `opencode run` can exit before its queued `stop` runs; the turn then ends at agent exit (AU26: a member whose launcher is not live counts as idle). (6) A tool call with no `tool.execute.after` keeps `tools_running` above 0 until the next `prompt` or `stop` call, the agent exit, the drain timeout or `down --now` (AU26, the safe side). OpenCode 1.19.6 fires `tool.execute.after` only when the tool returns: a tool that throws or whose permission is denied, a tool ended by Esc, a call that a `tool.execute.before` hook (the guard) denies after its `pre-tool`, and a command subtask whose agent is not found fire none (OpenCode tag `v1.19.6`, `packages/opencode/src/session/tools.ts:102-131` and `:398-424`, `packages/opencode/src/session/prompt.ts:307-393`, `packages/opencode/src/plugin/index.ts:284-297`). A call with no `callID` sends no `tool-end` either. (7) A `pre-tool` call that fails or is killed after 10 s still counts in the plugin; when the hook did not count it (AU56), its `tool-end` can lower the count of another running tool by one, as a lost `PreToolUse` does on Claude Code. (8) A start with `--pure` (no plugin, `fixtures/opencode/help.txt:38`), with the subcommand `attach` (`opencode attach <url>`) or with the subcommand `run` and `--attach` (`opencode run --attach <url>`; in both attach forms the plugin runs in a server without the member environment) sends no member events; its `launch.events_off` is `true` and the drain counts the member as busy until its agent exits, the drain timeout or `down --now` (AU26). OpenCode 1.19.6 takes `--attach` only for `run`: the TUI's help lists no `--attach`, and the parser ignores an option it does not know (FIX-24 plan, P12). `ws run` starts a member from its saved arguments, which hold no positional (AU30), so a member saved from `attach <url>` or `run --attach <url>` is restored as the TUI, which loads the plugin and sends member events; its start has `events_off` `false`. A saved `--pure` stays, so such a member is events off after a restore too. `chat.message` is not used: no PW4 fixture shows it firing. Evidence: PW4 (`fixtures/opencode/workspace/pw4.txt`, `fixtures/opencode/workspace/plugin-event-types.txt`). Author's call, 2026-11-22 (L2 plan; H1 re-plan, H3). | author |
| AU28 | Two new conformance capabilities (§8.1, C9), with ids that do not continue the base list A-1 to A-21: **WA-1 Resume** (pre-assign or capture the agent session id; resume by id) and **WA-2 Turn signals** (turn start and end, subagent start and end, dispatch block). | author |
| AU29 | `workspace.json` has `version = 1`. A zygo that finds a higher version refuses to change the workspace and prints `workspace state is from a newer zygo; run zygo update`. A `workspace.json` larger than 1 MiB counts as possibly from a newer zygo (AU84). | author |
| AU30 | Saved launch arguments (W§7.2 `member.launch.args`). zygo removes: its own session arguments; the agent's session arguments (Claude Code `-c`/`--continue`, `-r`/`--resume`, `--session-id` and its value, `--fork-session`, `--from-pr`, `--cloud`, `--remote` (the hidden alias of `--cloud`); OpenCode `-c`/`--continue`, `-s`/`--session` and its value, `--fork`); a flag with an optional value (`-r`/`--resume [value]`, `--from-pr [value]`, `--cloud [value]`, `--remote [value]`, and any other optional-value session flag in the pinned help) together with the next token when that token does not start with `-`; positional prompt text; `-p`/`--print`; prompt-text flags with their values (Claude Code `--system-prompt`, `--append-system-prompt`; OpenCode `--prompt`). It keeps `--system-prompt-file` and `--append-system-prompt-file` with the path only. Content flags (Claude Code `--agents`, `--mcp-config` (each value), `--json-schema`, `--plugin-url`; `fixtures/claude-code/help.txt:13`, `:126`, `:132`, `:166`) keep a value only when it names an existing file; zygo drops an inline value with its flag and adds the flag name to `member.dropped_flags`; `up` prints `zygo: pane <key>: <flag> had an inline value; not restored`. `--settings` is kept with its value only when the value is a file path; an inline JSON value is removed with the flag (W-C7). A value is inline JSON when its first non-space character is `{`; zygo adds `--settings` to `member.dropped_flags`, and `up` names it as it names a content flag. A kept path is stored as typed: zygo reads the file again at each launch and never passes it to the agent (AU59, main spec §8.5 Flags). File paths are stored absolute; a relative path in a loadout pane's `args` is taken relative to that pane's folder (`cwd`), for the file check and for the stored value. The rest is stored as typed. A flag that is not in the agent's table (`crates/zygo-workspace/src/args.rs`) is kept as typed, and the value rule of its agent decides whether the next token is its value (kept as typed with it) or a positional (removed). In a short cluster (`-abc`) every letter is looked up, the next token belongs to the last letter only, and a cluster that holds a removed letter is removed whole, with its value. Whether a letter before the last takes the rest of the token as its value, which ends the cluster, is the rule of the agent's parser. Claude Code (Commander): a letter that takes a value takes the rest of the token. OpenCode (the yargs-parser short-flag loop of OpenCode 1.19.6): a letter takes the text after a next `=`; the rest when the rest is `-`; the rest when the letter is alphabetic and the rest is a number; the rest from a next non-word character (that character included); else the letter is set with no value and the walk goes on. The loop does not see the last character of the token as a next character. So `zygo opencode -- -mc` is `-m` with no value and `-c`, a session argument, and is not saved. Claude Code: the table is complete for Claude Code 2.1.331, from its option list (`fixtures/claude-code/options-2.1.331.txt`), so a flag that is not in it takes no value: a registered one is a boolean (for example `--verbose`), and one that Claude Code does not register stops the start with `error: unknown option '<flag>'` and exit 1 before any session (`allowUnknownOption` is set only on its `remote-control` and `plugin test` subcommands), so zygo removes the next token and no prompt word reaches the state (W-C7). On a newer Claude Code, an option that is not in the table follows the same rule: it is kept as typed and takes no value, so a new option with a value is saved without its value. OpenCode: the value rule of the OpenCode 1.19.6 parser, the one function that `opencode_subcommand` also uses (W§7.2 `member.launch.events_off`): a flag that holds `=` takes no next token; a `--no-<x>` flag takes no next token; a boolean option of an OpenCode command (the `[boolean]` rows of `fixtures/opencode/help.txt` and the boolean options of each subcommand) takes the next token only when it is `true` or `false`; every other flag takes the next token unless that token starts with `-`. So `zygo opencode -- --zzunknown X` saves `--zzunknown X`, and `zygo opencode -- run --thinking "fix it"` saves `--thinking` only. The default command, which `ws run` restores, parses the tokens again with its own options, so its booleans take no value there, although the parse of the tokens before a subcommand gives them one (W§7.2). The OpenCode subcommand options whose value can hold a secret, a URL, user text or a path (`-p`/`--password`, `-u`/`--username`, `--attach`, `--token`, `--url`, `--env`, `--header`, `-f`/`--file`, `--title`, `--dir`, `--description`, `--path`, `--query`, `--params`, `--event`, `--cwd`) are in the table and are removed with their value (W-C7). The builder classifies flags from the pinned `--help` (`fixtures/claude-code/help.txt`, `fixtures/opencode/help.txt`), and, for Claude Code, from the option list of the 2.1.331 binary (`fixtures/claude-code/options-2.1.331.txt`, 139 options, written by `fixtures/claude-code/scan/mkfixture.py`). A hidden option with a value is kept with its value when the value is a plain setting (for example `--max-turns`, `--thinking`, `--channels`). It is removed with its value when the value can hold a secret, content, a prompt, a path to user content or a URL, or is data of one launch that a host, SDK or server sets (for example `--prefill`, `--lead-verify-key`, `--sdk-url`, `--workload`, `--forward-home-settings`). In doubt it is removed. A boolean is removed when it does one action and exits or a restore would do its action again (`--init-only`, `--init`, `--maintenance`, `--desktop`, `--bg`, `--tmux`, as `-p`/`--print`), when it is data of one launch set by a host, SDK, server or parent process (for example `--await-claim`, `--session-mirror`, `--reply-on-resume`), or when it belongs to a removed value flag (`--allow-other-models`, `--deep-link-origin`); the token after it stays its own argument. `--bare` is kept. The full lists with each class are in `docs/work/phase2/nodes/FIX-31.md` (D3, D4 and D7 tables). Known limit: a removed system-prompt text or inline value is not given again at resume. Known limit: the walk removes every positional, so an OpenCode subcommand (`run`, `attach`) and its positionals are not saved; `ws run` restores such a member as the TUI with the kept flags (`--attach` and its URL are removed, AU27 known limit 8). | author |
| AU31 | Work units keep the base behavior: the launcher folds and ends them at exit (`session_dir::fold_units`, `launcher.rs:731`; §8.2 step 6). `down` records the units that were open just before the stop as `member.closed_units`: for each `<agent>.begin.json` in the member's `$ZYGO_SESSION_DIR/work/` (`SessionDir::work`, `crates/zygo-core/src/session_dir.rs:78`), `down` calls `zygo_work::log::open_unit` (`crates/zygo-work/src/log.rs:127`) and takes `Begin.unit` (`log.rs:61`) when `Begin.stopped_at` (`log.rs:73`) is none (AU76). The resume note says that they were closed. | author |
| AU32 | Compaction at resume: `member.compact` has three states, written at each change (AU105): `set <w>` → `--compact <w>`; `cleared` → no `--compact`; absent (never changed in a member session) → `member.launch.compact`. Unlike the proxy's `Option<WindowArg>` (`crates/zygo-proxy/src/session.rs:88-91`), the change path knows whether a clear happened. `ws run` puts the value in `LaunchOpts.compact`, which the launcher sends to the proxy before the agent starts (`crates/zygo-launch/src/launcher.rs:452`). | author |
| AU33 | Withdrawn; replaced by AU62. | author |
| AU34 | `LaunchOpts` gets `member: Option<Member { ws_id, pane }>` (AU102) and an optional callback that the launcher calls after it creates the session directory (`launcher.rs:378-392`) and before the agent starts (`:639`). Signature (`SessionCallback`, `launcher.rs:86`): `FnOnce(&SessionDir, session_id: &str, program: &Path, member: Option<&Member>, env: &Env) -> anyhow::Result<()> + Send`, where `program` is the agent program that the launcher resolved, `member` is the member of this launch (`LaunchOpts.member`; a user `--settings` does not change it, AU59), and `env` is the launch environment after the §8.4 deny list, `probe_env = child_env(&opts.env, &cfg, &[])` (`launcher.rs:300`, passed at `:430`): what the agent gets apart from the names zygo sets; `cmd/member.rs` reads `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` from it (AU26). A member uses it to make the `results` symlink (AU16), apply the saved overrides (W§8.2 step 4) and write its member record (AU92). An error from the callback is logged and the launch goes on (C6), except the error `StopLaunch` (`zygo_launch::launcher::StopLaunch`): the launcher logs it, starts no agent and returns exit code 1. Only the `ws run` callback returns it, when the launcher of the conversation it resumes is live (W-C9, W§8.2 step 4). AU34 also adds a `received` flag in `signals.rs`: an `AtomicBool` that `handle` sets when SIGTERM or SIGHUP arrives, that `install` clears (`signals.rs:39-48`), and that nothing else resets. `Launcher` returns `LaunchOutcome { code, signal, spawned, received, ran }` (AU60, AU73); `ran` is the time from the agent's spawn to the end of its wait, none without a spawn. The new crate uses only public items: `zygo_work::repo::checkout` (`repo.rs:137`), not `find_checkout` (`pub(crate)`, `:119`), and the new return value, not `signals::stopped` (`pub(crate)`). A callback can make the launch a v1 launch: when it returns the error `NotMember` (`zygo_launch::launcher::NotMember`), the launcher logs it as any callback error and then sets `member` and `resume_note` to none before it builds the view, the settings and the agent environment. Only the hand-start callback returns it (W§8.4). | author |
| AU35 | Pane closed by hand: W§7.5. | author |
| AU36 | `up` never holds `lock` while tmux creates panes (W§8.1). Phase `building` marks that time. Member writes are allowed in every phase. | author |
| AU37 | `up --reset` or `up --loadout` on a running workspace exits 1 with `zygo: <name> is running; run zygo down -- <name> first`. On a stopped workspace, `--reset` lists each resumable conversation that it drops, with its agent session id, before it applies the loadout. | author |
| AU38 | A snapshot without `--force` runs only in phases `up` and `draining`. `zygo ws snapshot --force` also runs in `building` and `stopping`, never in `down`. Only `up` (W§8.1 step 12) and `down` (W§9.3 step 4) pass `--force`, and a `snapshot.pending` that a forced snapshot left holds `force`, so the writer that runs it later passes `--force` too (W§7.3). tmux hooks and member events never pass it themselves. | author |
| AU39 | The per-session zygo values are `ZYGO_SESSION`, `ZYGO_SESSION_DIR`, `ZYGO_AGENT`, `ZYGO_URL`, `ZYGO_REPO`, `ZYGO_WORK`, `ZYGO_MAIN_ROLE` (set by the launcher, `launcher.rs:612-618`), and `ZYGO_PANE`, `ZYGO_WORKSPACE`. AU64 says where they are removed. `ZYGO_CONFIG` (a user setting) is kept. | author |
| AU40 | Withdrawn (critic read 2, I11). | author |
| AU41 | Pane keys are assigned only under `lock` (W§7.4 step 4), so two concurrent writers cannot give one pane two keys. A hand-started member uses the same path (AU79). | author |
| AU42 | Limit, stated plainly: only `down` saves the closed-unit list and the interrupted subagents (W§9.3 step 8). After a crash, a reboot or a hand kill they are lost; overrides and the compaction override are not (OP12). The resume note then says `zygo did not stop this session cleanly; the list of closed units was not saved.` `clean_stop` exists only for this text. | author |
| AU43 | `busy` changes only on main-loop events (events without an `agent_id` key): `UserPromptSubmit` sets it; `Stop` and `StopFailure` clear it. Subagent events change only the running set. zygo does not read `Stop`'s `background_tasks` (AU85). | author |
| AU44 | Option names: internal verbs take the workspace id as `--ws-id <id>`; user verbs take the workspace name as the positional `<name>` or as `--name <name>`. No verb has a `--workspace` option. | author |
| AU45 | A fresh member takes its launch fields from the loadout pane (W§11): `kind`, `profile`, `args`, `yolo`, `passthrough`, `server`, `compact`, `config_dir`. A field that the pane does not set takes the default of `zygo claude` or `zygo opencode`. `ws run` lives in `crates/zygo/src/cmd/ws.rs`. It builds a `Launch` (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/mod.rs:87-108`) or `LaunchOpenCode` (`:112-123`) value and calls the existing `launch_opts` (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/claude.rs:29-50`, `crates/zygo/src/cmd/opencode.rs:29`, made `pub(crate)`). That function parses `compact` (a `String`, `mod.rs:99`, `:118`) with `parse_window` and sets `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from `config_dir`. | author |
| AU46 | Every pane that `up` creates starts through `zygo ws run`. A shell pane runs `ws run --shell`: it cleans its environment (AU64), sets `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` again (AU93), changes to the pane's cwd, and runs `exec $SHELL`. A pane that the operator splits later starts with tmux's default command and gets the session environment of AU64. | author |
| AU47 | Withdrawn (critic read 3, S2): no tombstones. A pane that is gone from the layout leaves a detached member (W§7.5). | author |
| AU48 | `up` writes a full `panes[]` entry (`key`, `kind`, `cwd`, `window`, `index`) for every pane that it creates, before tmux creates it (W§8.1 step 8). `ws run` always reads its cwd from `panes[]`. | author |
| AU49 | Withdrawn (critic read 3, S1). The note inputs are used once through `note_pending` (AU60, AU72). | author |
| AU50 | Withdrawn (critic read 2, I11). | author |
| AU51 | Withdrawn (critic read 2, I11). | author |
| AU52 | `zygo up --all` restores the workspaces with `down_all = true` and also the workspaces left with `phase = up` and no tmux session (an unclean stop, for example a reboot). This matches W-G3. | author |
| AU53 | `panes[].kind` comes from the member record, not from `#{pane_current_command}`: a pane key whose member record has state `running` or `resumable` has the member's agent kind; every other pane is `shell`. When `up` restores a pane as a shell because its member, in state `running` or `resumable`, has no `agent_session`, its step 8 write deletes that member record only when the record has no `pending.agent_session` and no pending start (W§10 "No agent session id saved"); the pane's kind is then `shell` in that write and in every later snapshot. A record with a pending start or a pending agent session id stays, and its pane's kind follows the record. When that `up` fails before its session exists, its undo puts each deleted record back, whole (W§8.1 step 9). | author |
| AU54 | tmux hook command: `up` writes each hook with the literal workspace id and the literal quoted zygo path; the hook reads no user option. Hook string: `run-shell -b "'<zygo path>' ws snapshot --ws-id <id> --event <hook>"`. Quote rule: the path is wrapped in `'`, and each `#` in it is doubled to `##` (`run-shell` expands formats). `up` checks the zygo path before it writes or creates anything (W§8.1 step 4) and the socket path right after the server exists (W§8.1 step 9). Every socket that zygo passes to tmux with `-S` gets the same check before its first tmux call: the saved `tmux_socket`, in every phase and before its AU62 check, because the dead-pane lines (W§8.1 step 12) and the line of AU100 quote it; and, when no socket is saved, the socket of `$TMUX` that the call falls back to (D18). By path: `up` (W§8.1 step 5, before steps 6 and 13; the `$TMUX` socket before the first tmux call of step 5 or 6), `zygo attach` and `down` (W§9.3 steps 2 and 3; the `$TMUX` socket before each tmux call of the detach rule, the guard, the session check and step 3) exit 2 with the line below; `up --all` skips that workspace with `zygo: <name> skipped: <the line below without its "zygo: ">` (W§8.1); `repair` refuses it before any tmux call, and `up` and `down` print that refusal once and exit 2; `zygo ws forget` checks the socket of its session check right before that call and exits 2 (W§12); `zygo ls` shows the row `unreachable` with no tmux call; a hand start prints `zygo: this agent is not saved by the workspace: <the line below without its "zygo: ">` and goes on as a v1 launch (W§8.4); a snapshot writes one `errors.log` line with that text and makes no tmux call (W§7.4 step 1): `zygo ws snapshot` exits 0, and a snapshot that the member wrapper, a verb, `up`, `down` or `zygo ws forget-pane` runs returns to its caller, which goes on. It exits 2 with `zygo: the zygo path or tmux socket path holds a quote, a backslash, a $ or a control character; workspaces cannot use it` when a path holds `'`, `"`, `\`, `$` or a control character. With those characters excluded, a single-quoted string reads the same in sh, bash, zsh and fish. | author |
| AU55 | tmux expands formats in `-c` (start directory) and `-n` (window name) (W-C8). `up` passes `-c <main worktree>` to `new-session` and `new-window`, with each `#` doubled to `##`, so later user splits open in the repository. `ws run` changes to the pane's own cwd itself (`std::env::set_current_dir`); a pane's saved cwd never reaches tmux. A saved window name is passed with each `#` doubled. A window whose `automatic-rename` is on has no saved name (`windows[].name` is none); at restore it keeps tmux's automatic name. A start directory, window name, layout or argument that ends with `;` is refused before the plan is written (W§8.1 step 7). | author |
| AU56 | `activity.json` (schema in W§9.1) is changed under an fs4 lock on `activity.lock` in the same session directory, with a 1 s bound: read, change, `write_atomic`, release. A hook that cannot get the lock in 1 s loses its change, writes one line to the session log and exits 0 (C6). A hook that does not get `lock` of `workspace.json` within its 1 s bound (W§7.3) writes one line to `errors.log` and exits 0; a state folder that is a link or not a folder gets that line in the session log instead. A hook does not change an `activity.json` that is a link or not a regular file. A hook also does not change an `activity.json` that cannot be read or parsed, is not a JSON object or is larger than 1 MiB (the drain counts it as busy, AU26), and it does not write a result larger than 1 MiB (`<path>: the new activity is <n> bytes, more than the 1048576 bytes that every reader accepts; not written`); the stored file stays as it is. Each failed change writes one line to the session log, by its class. A persistent condition, which every later event meets again, writes its line only for the events that come a few times per turn, `prompt`, `session` and `session-end` (the rule of AU84), so it writes one line per turn, not one per tool call; the tool, `stop` and subagent events write none. The persistent conditions: an `activity.json` that cannot be read, is larger than 1 MiB, does not parse (or is not a JSON object), is of a newer version, is a link or is not a regular file; an `activity.lock` that cannot be opened (a link, not a regular file, no permission); a failed write. A one-off failure writes its line on every event where it happens: the lock not taken within the 1 s bound, and a refused result larger than 1 MiB. Known limit: an `activity.json` that does not parse is never rewritten, and the member counts busy for the rest of the agent run (AU26). Known limit: a write that would pass 1 MiB is refused, so with hostile id sizes a `Stop` or a `SubagentStart` can be refused: the member stays busy until the next accepted `Stop`, or a background subagent is not seen by AU26 rule 2. The session log is not written when it is a link or not a regular file. | author |
| AU57 | `StopFailure` (in the pinned settings schema, `fixtures/claude-code/settings-schema.json:2228`) is registered for members and acts as `Stop`. PW7 covers a turn ended by Esc and a turn ended by an API error. | author |
| AU58 | Membership is decided once, by the launcher, and recorded in the session view (`member = { ws_id, pane }`). Hooks take membership only from that marker, never from `ZYGO_WORKSPACE`. `zygo hook prompt` reads `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` only as a gate before it looks for the marker (after a verb panicked, W§9.1 `UserPromptSubmit` row), as the OpenCode plugin does (W§9.1, OpenCode). For every launch that is not a member, the launcher removes `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` and `ZYGO_PANE` from the child environment. | author |
| AU59 | A user `--settings` does not change membership. zygo never passes a user `--settings` value to Claude Code: it merges each readable value into its own settings file and does not use a value it cannot read as a JSON object (main spec §8.5 Flags), so the agent always gets zygo's file, with the attribution keys and, unless the merged settings set `disableAllHooks: true`, the member hooks (W§9.1) and the guard hook. A member launch with `--settings` before any `--` therefore stays a member like any other: the launcher writes the marker into the session view, sets `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` and `ZYGO_PANE`, and passes the member to the session callback (AU34); the adapter registers the member hooks. A user `disableAllHooks: true` that wins in the merge stops every hook and the status line, zygo's included (main spec §8.5 Flags): such a member runs no member hook and writes no `activity.json`, and the drain counts it as busy until its agent exits, the drain timeout or `down --now` (AU26, hooks off). zygo prints only the main spec §8.5 Flags lines: when a value is merged, `zygo: your --settings is merged into zygo's settings file; zygo's attribution keys and hooks stay in force`, or, when the merged `disableAllHooks` is `true`, `zygo: your --settings is merged into zygo's settings file; zygo's attribution keys stay in force; your disableAllHooks stops every hook and the status line, zygo's included`; for each user `disableAllHooks` that is not `true` or `false`, `zygo: your --settings disableAllHooks value is <type>, not true or false; it was removed`; and, for each value it cannot read, one of the main spec §8.5 Flags lines for such a value: `zygo: your --settings value <value> could not be read as a JSON object and was not used: <error>` when an entry exists at the path, `zygo: your --settings value (not shown) is not a JSON object and names no file; it was not used` when no file is found, and `zygo: your --settings value (not shown) is not a JSON object and could not be opened as a file: <io error>; it was not used` for any other error. No workspace line is printed. The saved launch arguments keep `--settings` and its value as typed (AU30), so a resume reads the value again by the same rule. A `--settings` after `--` is not a flag (W4's walk: a flag value or a token after `--` is not a flag; `zygo_launch::claude_code::has_user_settings`). | author; FIX-21 round 3 (2026-11-22); FIX-24 (2026-11-23) |
| AU60 | Clear and keep. The commit of a pending start (AU92) of zygo session S clears `note_pending` in the member record. It also clears `closed_units` and `interrupted`, sets `clean_stop = false` and removes `saved_for`, unless `saved_for` is S: those values were saved by a `down` that stopped S (W§9.3 step 8) after the note of S was built, so they are kept for the next start, whatever the order of the two writes. Claude Code: the first main-loop `SessionStart` of S (the one whose member record's `pending` names S) prints the note, then commits; a later `SessionStart` (after `/clear` or a compaction) commits nothing and clears nothing. OpenCode: the plugin's `session` call commits (AU104). Every exit with `LaunchOutcome.spawned = false` keeps the member as it was. Member states: `running`, `resumable`, `ended`. There is no `resume_failed` state and no `--retry-failed`: every `up` tries each resumable member again. Known limit: a commit that does not get `lock` within its 1 s bound commits nothing; for OpenCode the next `session` call commits, else the member wrapper commits `pending` at agent exit (W§8.2 step 7) with no clear. | author |
| AU61 | A member record saves the absolute path of the agent program that the launcher found (`launcher.rs:274`) and, for a Claude Code member, the effective `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (absolute; from `--config-dir`, else the inherited value, else none). At a resume of a Claude Code member, `ws run` removes `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` from the launch environment and sets the saved value when there is one, so when the saved `member.config_dir` is none the resume's environment has no `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, even when the environment of `up` has one (PW16); an OpenCode member's environment keeps the variable as it is. At a resume, the agent program is found through `PATH` first, as today. Only when `PATH` has none and the saved path is an existing file is the saved path used: for Claude Code as the program path itself; for OpenCode, whose adapter has a fixed program name (`opencode.rs:402-404`), through the folder `<workspace cache>/<id>/program/<pane key>/`, which holds only a link named `opencode` to the saved path and is added at the end of `PATH`. `ws run` makes that folder before the launch, because the launcher finds the agent program on `PATH` before the callback (`launcher.rs:274`, `:407`), with the part rule of AU16 (each part made when missing, opened without following a link, set to mode `0700`); a symbolic link at `<id>`, `program` or `<pane key>` is not followed: `ws run` prints `zygo: <error>`, makes and deletes nothing, and the launch goes on without the folder. After it made the folder, `ws run` reads the state again: when the state is gone or has no record for the pane (a `zygo ws forget` or `zygo ws forget-pane` that ran after the read of W§8.2 step 1), it removes the folder it made (and, when the state is gone, the empty `program` and `<id>` folders), prints the line of step 1 (`zygo: no workspace with id <id>`, or `zygo: pane <key> has no member record; started a shell`) and opens the shell with no launch (W§12). `zygo ws forget` and `zygo ws forget-pane` delete the folder (W§12). The folder of the saved program is never added to `PATH`, and the record keeps the saved path. A fresh start uses `PATH` only. Missing cwd: zygo does not resume the conversation in another directory. The pane opens a shell in the main worktree and prints `zygo: <cwd> is gone; agent session <id> not resumed; to resume it: cd <dir> && <resume command>`, with the resume command of W§8.2 step 7. Printed resume command: the `zygo` launch that reproduces the `ws run` resume of the member record. One function makes it from the values that `ws run` gives `launch_opts` (`ws.rs` `launch_of`, `launch_open_code_of`) and from the launcher's own resume arguments, so the two cannot differ. For a Claude Code member it is `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<dir> zygo claude<flags> -- <args>` with a config dir, else `env -u CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR zygo claude<flags> -- <args>`, the environment that `ws run` gives a resume, so the copied command finds the conversation (PW16); for an OpenCode member `zygo opencode<flags> -- <args>`. `<flags>` are the flags that reproduce the `ws run` launch, each one only when it is set, in this order: ` --profile <profile>`; ` --passthrough` (Claude Code); ` --server <server>`, the saved value as it is (W-C7: the state holds no user info); ` --compact <window>`, the effective compaction of AU32 (the `set` override, else the saved `--compact`; a `cleared` override gives no flag, because `ws run` then gives `launch_opts` no `--compact` either); ` --yolo`. `<args>` are the agent arguments of the resume as the launcher builds them (`launcher.rs` `resume_argv`): the saved `launch.args` with the adapter's resume token, `--resume=<id>` (Claude Code) or `--session=<id>` (OpenCode), at the place where the launcher inserts it (first; for `opencode run`, right after `run`). The missing-cwd line takes the saved `member.config_dir`, the W§8.2 step 7 lines the config dir of the launch. Each path, each flag value, each agent argument and the agent session id in a printed command (`cd <dir>`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<dir>`, `--profile <profile>`, `'--model'`, the `<id>` of `--resume=<id>` and `--session=<id>`) is one shell word made from the raw bytes: in single quotes, a `'` written as `'\''`; when the value holds a control character or a bidi control (C6) or is not UTF-8, in `$'…'` with each such byte as `\xHH`, `\` as `\\` and `'` as `\'`, so the line stays one line and names the true value. The text before the command shows the values escaped (C6). PW16 checks resume by id from another cwd and another config dir. | author |
| AU62 | tmux server identity: `workspace.json` stores `tmux_socket`, the absolute socket path, always. Every tmux call for a workspace passes `-S <tmux_socket>`, so neither `$TMUX` nor `TMUX_TMPDIR` can redirect it. At creation, `up` uses the server of `$TMUX` when set, else tmux's default resolution, and reads the path after the server exists with `display-message -p -t =zygo-<name> '#{socket_path}'`. `up` writes the socket of `$TMUX` into `tmux_socket` before `new-session` (W§8.1 step 8). Before it uses `-S`, zygo checks that the socket and its parent directory are owned by the current uid and that the parent directory has mode `0700`; else it exits 1 naming the path. A missing parent directory is created with mode `0700`. A hand start checks the socket of `$TMUX` by this rule before its `display` call (W§8.4). | author |
| AU63 | Before `attach`, `switch-client`, `kill-session` or any `down` step, zygo reads the session option `@zygo-ws` of `zygo-<name>`, validates it (AU84) and compares it with the workspace id. A mismatch stops the verb: `zygo: tmux session zygo-<name> does not belong to this workspace; rename it or run zygo ws forget -- <name>` (exit 1). | author |
| AU64 | Environment of tmux and of `ws run`. Provider keys are **not** removed: the in-process proxy of each pane's launcher reads `env:` keys from its own environment (`SystemKeys`, `crates/zygo-proxy/src/provider.rs:19-25`), and the launcher's own filter already keeps them out of the agent child (`crates/zygo-launch/src/env.rs:21-23`). `up` (for the tmux command) and `ws run` (for itself) remove: the per-session `ZYGO_*` values (AU39); `ZYGO_RELAUNCHED`, the mark of a relaunched `ws run` (W§8.2 step 1a); the Claude Code markers `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID` (`fixtures/claude-code/settings-schema.json:540`, `:645`, `:1180`, `:637`); and, when `ZYGO_SESSION` is set (the caller runs inside a zygo agent), the values that the launcher sets for its agent child: `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS` (holds `X-Zygo-Token` in passthrough), `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_FABLE_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_OPUS_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_SONNET_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_GATEWAY_HINT_HEADERS`, `DISABLE_LOGIN_COMMAND`, `DISABLE_LOGOUT_COMMAND` (`crates/zygo-launch/src/claude_code.rs:129-150`), `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT` (holds the session token), `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` (`crates/zygo-launch/src/opencode.rs:465`). The workspace session hides the same names with `set-environment -r` (`-u` would only remove the session entry and let a global value through). The launcher denylist gains the four Claude Code markers (base change, W§15). Known limit: an agent has no provider env keys and no `ZYGO_TEAM_TOKEN` (the launcher removed them, `env.rs:21-23`), so a tmux server that an agent's `up` starts has none either; panes then need keychain keys (and the team token from the keychain in serve mode), or the operator runs `up` from a shell. `up` prints one line when it starts a new server with `ZYGO_SESSION` set. | author |
| AU65 | Detached `down` started by an agent must outlive the agent's Bash tool, also in a sandbox (PW15). PW15 failed (2026-11-22): with the Claude Code sandbox on, a process started with `setsid` from the Bash tool ends with the tool call. Fallback in effect: the MCP tool `workspace_down` on the zygo MCP server (feature toggle `[features] workspace_tool`, default off; a profile cannot set it), listed only in a member session with the toggle on. Input `now` (bool, optional). It runs `<zygo> down -- <name>` (`<zygo> down --now -- <name>` with `now`) with `ZYGO_SESSION` set, so the AU18 detach applies; the name comes after `--`, so a name that starts with `-` (AU84) is read as the name and no name is refused. It waits at most 5 s and returns the output line; a non-zero exit or the timeout is a tool error. The zygo MCP server runs outside the agent's sandbox and lives for the whole agent session (confirmed 2026-11-23: W§17 PW15 note of E2). The operator allowed "an internal tool if need be". | author |
| AU66 | Withdrawn (critic read 2, I11). | author |
| AU67 | Repair from `building` (W§8.1 step 5): when `op.lock` is free, `phase = building` and the tmux session exists, `up` creates each pane of `panes[]` whose key is not in the session, as in W§8.1 step 11, with the build hold: `remain-on-exit on` on each window of the session and, for a window that it creates, in the same `new-window -a -t =zygo-<name>:{end}` invocation. Then, as `up` does in W§8.1 step 12, in the same order and with the same best-effort rules, it ends the hold on every window of the session, reads `#{pane_dead}` and `#{pane_dead_status}` of each pane with a pane key, sets `phase = up`, removes `build_plan` and `fresh_members`, and prints the W§8.1 step 12 line of each dead pane (a pane that died during the hold of the stopped `up` or of this repair). When the repair stops on an error before that write, it reads the windows of the session again and sets `off` on each window that it set `on` and on each window that it created, best effort, and then fails; `phase` stays `building`. The saved `tmux_socket` of the state passes the AU54 check before any use. When the session does not exist, one write sets `phase = down` and acts by `build_plan`: `loadout` clears `windows[]` and `panes[]`, deletes each `fresh_members` record that has no agent session id (`agent_session` or `pending.agent_session`) and adds 1 to `snapshot_seq`; `restore` keeps the layout and deletes no record; none (a file of an older zygo) keeps everything and prints `zygo: <name>: the state has no build plan (an older zygo wrote it); its layout and members stay`. The same write removes `build_plan` and `fresh_members`. No other member record is deleted. | author |
| AU68 | Session options set by `up` on the workspace session only: `destroy-unattached off` and `remain-on-exit off`. `up` sets `destroy-unattached off` in the same tmux invocation as `new-session` (W§8.1 step 9), and again in step 10. `remain-on-exit` is a window option: `on` in the invocation that creates each window (W§8.1 steps 9 and 11, the build hold), and `off` on each built window when the build ends, before the `phase = up` write (step 12); a repair from `building` (AU67) sets `on` on each window of the session while it builds and `off` on each window before its `phase = up` write, or when it stops on an error. When a later `up` attaches to the live session (W§8.1 step 6), it first sets `off` on each window of the session that holds a pane with a valid `@zygo-pane` (read in the same `list-panes` call), best effort; a window that the user made, with no zygo pane, is not changed. `up --all` does the same for each live session that holds the workspace's `@zygo-ws`, under `op.lock`; a held `op.lock` skips it, so an `up` or `down` that is still building keeps its hold. So a hold that a failed release left ends at the next `up` or `up --all`. Known limit: until then, a pane that exits in such a window stays as a dead pane, and tmux fires `pane-died` for it, not `pane-exited`. | author |
| AU69 | Sweep additions (`crates/zygo-results/src/sweep.rs`). A session directory whose `results` is a symlink whose target, made canonical, is under the canonical `workspace_cache_dir` is deleted by the age rule of the base sweep (§10.1: 24 h after the newest change of the directory and its direct entries), when it is not live and is still not live when checked again just before the removal: the link first, then the directory. When the link target does not exist (after `zygo ws forget` or `zygo ws forget-pane`), the link text decides: a text with a `..` component keeps the directory (the kernel takes `..` after a symlink from that symlink's target, so the text alone cannot show where it points); any other text is made absolute against the session directory and its existing part made canonical; a part on that path that is itself a link whose target is missing (or any entry that cannot be made canonical) keeps the directory; under the canonical `workspace_cache_dir`, the same rule applies. The link target is never touched (AU16); the launcher callback makes the link as an absolute path under `workspace_cache_dir`. The sweep never deletes a workspace results folder (`results/<pane key>/` under `workspace_cache_dir`) or anything in it, by age, by member record or for any other reason; only the user commands `zygo ws forget` and `zygo ws forget-pane` delete one (W§12). The same two commands are the only ones that delete a program folder `<workspace cache>/<id>/program/<pane key>/` (AU61); the sweep does not read it. Test: `w18_61_the_sweep_deletes_no_workspace_result` (`crates/zygo-results/tests/results.rs`). | author |
| AU70 | Permission flags are shown. The bypass forms are `--dangerously-skip-permissions` (`fixtures/claude-code/help.txt:70`), `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` (`:18`), `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` (`:149-151`), and a saved `yolo = true`. Every `up` prints, for each resumed member, the bypass and permission flags in its saved arguments. There is no gate and no `--yolo` for `up`. Accepted limit: an agent of the same user can edit `workspace.json` and change the saved arguments, as it can read the session token file (§8.4). | author |
| AU71 | `zygo ws ...` (all `ws` subcommands) skips the results sweep that `crates/zygo/src/cmd/mod.rs:548-551` runs for every verb except `hook` and `mcp`. Reason: snapshots run on layout events, and a sweep on each one costs a directory scan. | author |
| AU72 | The resume note inputs reach the session view through `LaunchOpts.resume_note`. The launcher copies them into the `SessionView` value that it builds at `launcher.rs:566-588` (`:586`), before `view.save` (`:589-592`) and before the agent's spawn (`:666-671`).
