A callback write to the view would be lost, because `view.save` writes the whole view. The inputs carry `agent_session`: the agent session id that `ws run` resumes, or none for a fresh start (W§9.4). | author |
| AU73 | `LaunchOutcome.signal` is the signal that ended the agent, or none. `exit_code` (`launcher.rs:778-790`) maps a death by signal n to 128 + n, so a real exit 130 and a death by SIGINT look the same; AU17 and W§8.2 step 7 use the separate signal. When a member becomes `ended` with a non-zero exit, `ws run` prints `zygo: <kind> exited <code>; agent session <id> is kept by the agent; to resume it: <resume command>` (W§8.2 step 7). | author |
| AU74 | `down` reads, saves or signals a member only when its launcher is live (W§5). A dead launcher's `pid` file can name an unrelated process. | author |
| AU75 | Repair after a `down` that died in `stopping` (W§8.1 step 5): if the session is gone, `phase = down`. If the session exists and no member launcher is live, `up` (or `down`) completes the stop: it kills the session (after AU63), then sets `phase = down`. If some launcher is still live, set `phase = up`, and for each member whose launcher is live, set `clean_stop = false`, clear `closed_units` and `interrupted`, and remove `saved_for`. | author |
| AU76 | AU31 records a unit only when its `Begin.stopped_at` is none. A unit with `stopped_at` set was ended by its `SubagentStop`; `fold_units` uses that time as its end (`crates/zygo-launch/src/session_dir.rs:87`). | author |
| AU77 | Bare repository with worktrees. `Checkout.main_root` is the first `worktree` entry of `git worktree list --porcelain` (`crates/zygo-work/src/repo.rs:133`); in a bare repository that entry is the bare directory, which has no working tree. The workspace crate reads the list itself and skips entries marked `bare`. The first non-bare entry is the **main worktree**: it gives the name (AU2) and the default cwd. With no non-bare entry, `up` exits 2 with `zygo up needs a worktree; this repository is bare`. `repo.rs` does not change. | author |
| AU78 | Schema change across versions. Every struct of `workspace.json` keeps the fields it does not know in a flattened map (`#[serde(flatten)] extra`), and a write keeps them. Within `version = 1` a change may only add fields. Any other change raises `version` (AU29). | author |
| AU79 | A hand-started member that creates a pane key (W§8.4) uses the locked path of W§7.4 step 4. `assign_key` changes only a state that exists (`Store::update_existing`): when `ws forget` removed the state after the read, it sets no key and makes no folder (`no workspace state`). | author |
| AU80 | `down --all` and `up --all` skip a workspace whose `workspace.json` does not parse (or has a newer version), report it, continue with the others, and exit 1 at the end. | author |
| AU81 | Withdrawn (critic read 2, I2). | author |
| AU82 | Withdrawn (critic read 2, M3). | author |
| AU83 | Blocks and `op.lock`. The dispatch block and the stopping block apply only while a `down` process holds `op.lock`. The hook first reads `phase` without a lock; only when it is `draining` or `stopping` does it try `op.lock` without waiting; if it gets it (no `down` runs), it releases it at once and blocks nothing. `up` and `down` retry `op.lock` for 200 ms before they report it held, so a hook's short hold does not make them fail. | author |
| AU84 | zygo validates at each read: pane keys against `^k[a-z2-7]{8}$`; workspace ids against `^[0-9a-f]{16}$`; `name` against `^[a-z0-9-]{1,32}$`; `tmux_session` against `^zygo-[a-z0-9-]{1,32}$` and equal to `zygo-<name>`. Sources: tmux options `@zygo-pane` and `@zygo-ws`, `workspace.json`, `--ws-id`, `--pane`. A value that does not match is refused: a hook or snapshot writes one line to `errors.log` and changes nothing; a verb exits 1. Exception: `zygo ws snapshot` with an invalid `--ws-id` has no state directory to log into, so it prints `zygo: invalid workspace id <id>` on stderr, changes nothing and exits 0. A hook that reads a workspace id that does not match from the session view has no state directory either: it writes the line to the session log (`session.log` in `ZYGO_SESSION_DIR`) and changes nothing. Each key of `fresh_members` is checked as a pane key. A `workspace.json` larger than 1 MiB is refused as `state file too large`: every reader reads at most 1 MiB + 1 byte of it, so no state read allocates more. Such a file may be from a newer zygo (AU29), so no command changes, renames or removes it: `up`, `up --reset`, `ws forget <id>` and `ws forget-pane` exit 1 with `zygo: <path>: state file too large; it may be from a newer zygo, so zygo does not change it`, `up --all` skips it (AU80), and hooks and snapshots change nothing. It is found by its id only, because its name is not read; `zygo workspace` gives that text, and `ls` shows its row by id as `unreadable`. A member hook writes the line of a session-view marker value that does not match, or of a session view that it cannot read, only for the events that come a few times per turn: `prompt`, `session`, `session-end` and the Claude Code `SessionStart`; the tool, `stop` and subagent events and the OpenCode per-request `session-start` change nothing and write no line. | author |
| AU85 | Withdrawn premise PW8: zygo does not read `Stop`'s `background_tasks`. A background subagent stays in the running set until its `SubagentStop`. | author |
| AU86 | Stopping is final. From W§9.3 step 7 (`phase = stopping`): member hooks block `UserPromptSubmit` with `{"decision":"block","reason":"zygo: workspace is stopping"}` and deny `Agent` with the same reason (AU83 applies); `down` ignores SIGINT, SIGHUP and SIGTERM; `down` never goes back to draining. For OpenCode, the stopping block does not apply to the `prompt` call: the plugin sends it on the first `busy` status, after OpenCode has taken the prompt and started the turn, and a plugin `event` handler cannot refuse an event. The `pre-tool` deny on `task` and the drain still apply. A member's `/zygo:` line whose verb panics passes to the model during `stopping` too (C6; W§9.1 `UserPromptSubmit` row): the turn is marked busy, and `down` stops it as any running agent. | author |
| AU87 | "Session gone" needs two facts. (1) tmux gives an explicit answer, read under `LC_ALL=C`: `can't find session`, `no such session:`, `no server running on`, `server exited unexpectedly`, `error connecting to <path> (No such file or directory)` / `(Connection refused)`, or `no current target` (a server that runs with no session, `exit-empty off`; tmux 3.2a and 3.7c, W7 probe, 2026-11-22) (the first and fifth observed on tmux 3.7c with a private server; PW6 confirms on the pinned version; tmux 3.7c prints `no such session:` for `show-options` on a missing session, W3 plan probe, 2026-11-22; `has-session` right after the last session is killed prints `server exited unexpectedly` in 28 of 40 runs and `no server running on` in the other 12, W3 review probe, 2026-11-22). (2) No member launcher is live: for each member, `session_dir::live` on `sessions/<zygo_session>` and on `sessions/<pending.zygo_session>` (AU92) is false. If (1) holds and (2) fails, the answer is "unreachable", not "gone": the verb exits 1 with `zygo: <name> has live agents (pane <key>) but its tmux session cannot be reached at <socket>`, and nothing changes. Any other tmux error (a timeout, a permission error, another message) makes the verb exit 1, or the snapshot write one line to `errors.log`, and changes nothing. W§8.1 step 7 never resumes a member whose launcher is live. | author |
| AU88 | `ws run` and the wrapper of a hand-started member do not change the handling of SIGHUP, SIGTERM, SIGINT or SIGQUIT before or while `Launcher::run` runs. The launcher installs one handler for the four signals before it creates the session (`signals::install`, `launcher.rs:321`). While the agent runs, the handler forwards SIGTERM and SIGHUP to the agent and records them (`LaunchOutcome.received`), and SIGINT and SIGQUIT do nothing in the launcher (the agent gets them from the terminal); so a hangup of the pane while the agent runs does not stop the wrapper, and the wrapper writes the exit record after `Launcher::run` returns. A signal whose handling is already ignored gets no handler (`signals.rs:151-153`), and the agent inherits the ignored disposition at its spawn (`launcher.rs:639`); so the wrapper ignores no signal itself. When `Launcher::run` returns, the handling that was in place before it is back (`Installed::drop`, `signals.rs:50-57`). `ws run` sets the default handling of the four signals right before `exec`, so the shell does not inherit an ignored disposition. Known limits: (1) one of the four signals that arrives before the launcher installs its handler stops `ws run` by the default action before the session callback, so the member record does not change; (2) one that arrives after `Launcher::run` returns and before the exit record is written stops `ws run` by the default action, so the exit record may not be written: the record then keeps `pending` or a `zygo_session` of a launcher that is no longer live, which `Store::live_launcher` (`state.rs:584`) treats as not live, and the next `up` treats the pane as dead (AU87). A signal that arrives after the exit record is written does not change the record. zygo adds no other signal handling for these windows. | author |
| AU89 | Snapshot shrink rule. A snapshot whose read lacks a key that `panes[]` holds waits 2 s in its own process and reads the panes again. It uses a read only when the session still exists and the read shows the same tmux panes (the same set of pane ids) as the read before it. When they differ, it waits 2 s and reads again, at most two more times; then it sets `snapshot.pending` (W§7.3) and does not write. A teardown (`kill-session`, server death) ends with the session gone, so its shrinking snapshots never write (PW6). In a hook process the pending snapshot runs in a detached child, so no hook waits for the 2 s (W§7.3). | author |
| AU90 | No snapshot on focus changes: zygo sets no `after-select-pane` or `after-select-window` hook. The active window and pane come from the last snapshot, and `down` takes a final snapshot (W§9.3 step 4). | author |
| AU91 | The proxy session status (`GET /v1/zygo/sessions/{id}/status`, §7.2; `SessionStatus`, `crates/zygo-proxy/src/client.rs:39-91`, built by `SessionState::status`, `crates/zygo-proxy/src/session.rs:311-357`) gains two fields: `requests_in_flight` (model requests accepted on the forward path and not yet ended, a streamed body included: `InFlight::begin` right after `accept`, `crates/zygo-proxy/src/forward.rs:379-380`, until the `InFlight` value drops, `session.rs:375-395`) and `last_request_end` (RFC 3339 time, or none). The fields are added only, so older clients ignore them. The same route serves local and serve mode, so a serve-mode member uses its team server's status. Before AU91 the proxy had no per-session request state: every session route calls `accept`, which only checks that the session is mapped and active (`session.rs:593-599`; the status route calls it at `crates/zygo-proxy/src/routes.rs:225`), the heartbeat only sets `last_heartbeat` (`session.rs:602-609`), and the only in-flight count was a global byte semaphore (`crates/zygo-proxy/src/server.rs:110-113`). `expires_at` cannot give it either: it is always empty, because no session expires by idle time (`client.rs:47-51`, FIX-18). Approved by the operator (OP13). | author |
| AU92 | Member record at start. The launcher callback writes, in one write under `lock`: `member.pending = { zygo_session, agent_session (pre-assigned; for a hand start with an own session argument, that argument with `own_session = true`, W§8.4; or none), started_at }`; `member.launch` (AU30-filtered, with `events_off` computed from the arguments of this start, W§7.2), `member.config_dir`, `member.dropped_flags` (the saved list stays when the filtered launch, apart from `events_off`, equals the saved launch; else the filter's list replaces it); and `member.program` from the callback's `program` argument, refreshed at each start. `pending` is committed (moved into `zygo_session`, and into `agent_session` only when `pending.agent_session` is set, with `state = running`; AU60 clears) by the first main-loop `SessionStart` of that zygo session (which proves the spawn), for OpenCode by the plugin's `session` call while `pending` names that zygo session (AU104), or by the wrapper when `LaunchOutcome.spawned = true`, whichever comes first. The wrapper's commit never moves an `agent_session` with `own_session = true`: an id that the user typed is saved only when a member hook or the plugin confirms the start, so an id that does not exist is never saved. The commit never takes `agent_session` from the hook payload; a payload id is recorded after it (W§7.2). With `spawned = false` the wrapper deletes `pending`; the other fields of the write describe the launch as typed and may stay. Live checks (AU74, AU87, W§8.4) consider both `zygo_session` and `pending.zygo_session`. Exits before the callback (`launcher.rs:227`, `:239`, `:251`, `:255`, `:262`, `:276`, `:335`, `:343`, `:355`, `:360`, `:364`, `:385`, `:390`) write nothing. Exits after the callback and before the spawn (`:416`, `:450`, `:454`, `:458`, `:465`, `:559`, `:589`, `:595`, `:600`, `:632`, `:644`, `:648`) leave `pending`, which the wrapper deletes. Each commit of a pending start (the member `SessionStart`, the OpenCode `session` call, the wrapper's exit commit) sets `first_started_at = pending.started_at` when `first_started_at` is absent or 0 and `pending.started_at` is not 0. The start write itself does not set it: a start that never spawns (`spawned = false`) leaves no trace. | author |
| AU93 | `ws run` sets `ZYGO_WORKSPACE=<id>` again right before `exec $SHELL` (it removed it with AU64), so a `zygo claude` that the operator starts in that shell passes W§8.4. It does not set `ZYGO_PANE`; the pane key comes from `@zygo-pane`. | author |
| AU94 | A hand-started launch on a pane key is refused (exit 1, nothing started, the record unchanged) when the key's record has a live launcher (`zygo: pane <key> holds session <id>, whose launcher is still running; zygo starts no second agent on it`, where `<id>` is the record's `agent_session`, else the zygo session of the live launcher), or when the record is `running` or `resumable` with an `agent_session` and the launch's own session argument is not that id (`zygo: pane <key> holds resumable session <id>; to resume it: <command>; or run zygo ws forget-pane <key>`, where `<command>` is the AU61 resume command of that record, the one `ws run` prints, W§8.2 step 7). The launch's own session argument is the value of Claude Code `--resume`/`-r` or `--session-id`, or OpenCode `--session`/`-s`, read by AU30's walk. A launch of the record's agent kind whose own session argument is the record's `agent_session` is the resume that zygo printed: it is accepted, and the record keeps its fields (`agent_session`, the saved `launch` and `dropped_flags`, `overrides`, `compact`, note fields); the start write sets only state `running`, `pending`, `config_dir`, `program` and `launch.events_off`, which it computes from the arguments typed for this start (W§7.2). A launch of another agent kind is never that resume: on a `running` or `resumable` record with an `agent_session` it gets the refusal above with the record's own resume command, and the record does not change. Any other record (`ended`, or no agent session and no live launcher) is replaced by a new one. The same checks run again in the launcher callback (W§8.4). | author |
| AU95 | When `ZYGO_SESSION` is set, `up` acts as with `--no-attach`. Reason: an agent's tool has no terminal to attach. | author |
| AU96 | Window size. `windows[]` saves `width` and `height` (`#{window_width}`, `#{window_height}`). `up` creates the first window with `new-session -x <width> -y <height>` and sets the session option `default-size <width>x<height>` before each later `new-window` (PW6). On tmux 3.2, `new-session -d -x -y` sizes the first window from the client that runs the command (80x23 when it has no terminal; 3.2a `cmd-new-session.c:272`; tmux 3.3 and later give a detached session no client, `cmd-new-session.c:283-284`). So `up` follows `new-session` with `resize-window -x <width> -y <height>` on the first window. After the last `select-layout` of W§8.1 step 11, `up` unsets that window's `window-size` option (`set-option -w -u`), because `resize-window` sets it to `manual`. Then the window follows an attached client like the other windows. A loadout window has no size: `up` creates it at 200×50. After the build every window follows the client that attaches, because the first window's `window-size` is unset and the later windows never had it set. A saved `width` or `height` outside 1..=10000 (tmux's window maximum), or a window name with a control character, makes `up` exit 1 before it writes the plan. When a `split-window` fails, zygo removes that pane's `panes[]` entry, so its member is detached and stays `resumable` (W§7.5); it prints `zygo: pane <key> not built (no space); agent session <id> is detached`, goes on, and ends with `phase = up`. | author |
| AU97 | Output of a detached `down`: `down.log` in the workspace state directory for one workspace; `~/.local/state/zygo/down-all.log` for `--all`. Each is at most 64 KiB: before it starts the detached process, and only while `op.lock` of each target is free (no other detached `down` of it is live), zygo cuts a log larger than 32 KiB in place to its newest whole lines within 32 KiB (when the last 32 KiB hold no line start, it keeps them as they are from their first UTF-8 character boundary; a log with content is never cut to empty), under `op.lock` and through one file handle, never by rename. With a lock held, the log is only appended to. | author |
| AU98 | A `--server` URL (or loadout `server`) that holds user info (`user:pass@`) cannot be saved: the launch stays a v1 launch and prints `zygo: --server holds user info; this agent is not saved by the workspace`; `zygo config check` reports such a loadout pane. In a loadout pane, `up` builds that pane as a shell pane (`ws run --shell`) with no member record, prints the line above, goes on, and exits 0. | author |
| AU99 | `panes[]` is read with one `list-panes` whose format puts `#{pane_current_path}` last. zygo splits each line into the fixed number of fields from the left, the last field taking the rest, and refuses a line with fewer fields (one `errors.log` line, no write). Known limit: a window that a user named with `new-window -n` on tmux 3.2 and whose name holds backslash escapes can read back changed, because zygo names its own windows with `rename-window` and decodes every name the same way. | author |
| AU100 | Inside tmux, when the socket of `$TMUX` is not `tmux_socket`, `up` and `attach` do not run `switch-client`. They print `zygo: <name> runs on another tmux server; attach with: tmux -S '<socket>' attach -t =zygo-<name>` and exit 0. | author |
| AU101 | Withdrawn by the operator; OP12 replaces it. | author |
| AU102 | Membership is decided in the cmd layer (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/`) and passed as `LaunchOpts.member = Some(Member { ws_id, pane })`. `ws run` passes it explicitly and skips the environment and tmux detection; only a hand start uses W§8.4. The user-info check (AU98) runs before any pane key or member record is written. A `--settings` in the arguments does not change membership (AU59): a loadout pane whose arguments hold it is a member like any other. With `member = None` the launcher behaves as v1 (AU58). | author |
| AU103 | Crate graph. `zygo-workspace` holds identity, state, locks, snapshot, loadout, drain, argument filter and mux, and depends on `zygo-core`, `zygo-config`, `zygo-work` and `zygo-proxy` only (the proxy client for AU91). It does not depend on `zygo-launch` or `zygo-hooks`. `ws run`, the member wrapper and the membership decision live in `crates/zygo/src/cmd/` (AU45, AU102). `zygo-hooks` depends on `zygo-workspace` (member records, `activity.json`). No cycle: today `zygo-hooks` depends on config, core, proxy, results, usage and work; `zygo-launch` depends on `zygo-hooks` and on `zygo-workspace` (the AU30 table and parser for the session flags, node L2); neither is a dependency of the four crates that `zygo-workspace` uses (`crates/*/Cargo.toml`). | author |
| AU104 | OpenCode member events. The generated plugin (`crates/zygo-launch/assets/opencode-plugin.js`) calls `zygo hook workspace --agent opencode --event session`. With `{"cwd": <directory>}` (no session id) the hook commits the pending start of its zygo session (AU92) and clears by AU60; with `{"sessionID": <id>, "cwd": <directory>}` it does the same, then writes `member.agent_session` when the id differs (a snapshot follows, W§7.4) and applies the note rule of W§9.4. A payload with no session id, an empty one or one that is not a string keeps the stored `member.agent_session`. The `session` call does not change `activity.json`. The plugin's first call, at load, is a `session` call with no session id. Which sessions the plugin sends: AU27. The resume note reaches the agent through the plugin's `experimental.chat.system.transform` hook, which calls `session-start` on each model request of the main role, so the note is in the system text of every request while its inputs are in the session view. `busy` comes from the plugin's `prompt` and `stop` calls (AU27). The OpenCode cells of WA-1 and WA-2 are recorded as partial until PW4 passes. Known limit: a `session` call that does not get `lock` within its 1 s bound commits nothing; the next `session` call commits, else the member wrapper commits at agent exit with no clear (AU60). Known limit: the plugin counts a `session` call with an id as sent when it queues it, so if that call fails (it does not get `lock` within its bound, `zygo` exits non-zero, the spawn fails, or the plugin kills it after 10 s), the id is not recorded until the main session changes; the member wrapper's commit at agent exit (W§8.2 step 7, node M2) keeps the id that the member had. | author |
| AU105 | Override mirror (OP12). The code paths that change a member's saved values: (a) every session setting verb, CLI and `/zygo:` alike, in `set_session` (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/verbs.rs:266-324`), the one function that writes `overrides.json` (`Overrides::save` at `:298`, and the rollback `restore` at `:168-182` when the proxy refuses a `redaction content` change); `set_session` reads the old `overrides.json` once, before any change and for every session, member or not, by the session-file rule (`read_session_file`, `crates/zygo-hooks/src/workspace.rs:94`: the open does not follow a link and does not wait on a FIFO, and only a regular file is read): a link, a FIFO or another entry that is not a regular file stops the setting verb with exit 1 and one line, `zygo <verb>: cannot read <full path>: <reason>`, with no PATCH and no save (base spec §17.1); the new overrides are built from the bytes of that read (`Overrides::parse`, `crates/zygo-core/src/overrides.rs:33`), so the file is not opened again; (b) the `zygo compact` verb and `/zygo:compact` (`run`, `crates/zygo/src/cmd/compact.rs:60-111`), with `zygo compact --session <id>` writing the record of the session folder `<sessions dir>/<id>`, also when `ZYGO_SESSION_DIR` names another folder; an id that is empty or has a byte outside `[A-Za-z0-9]` (the rule of `Store::live_launcher`) is refused before any read (`compact failed: invalid session id <id>`, exit 2). After the verb's outcome is final (after the rollback check, so a rolled-back change is not mirrored; a `redaction content` PATCH lost to a transport error or a timeout keeps the new `overrides.json`, so it is mirrored), the verb reads the target session's membership marker by the hook rule (AU58, AU84; `verb_member`, `workspace.rs:467`): the slash form first needs `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` set and not empty, the gate of the prompt hook, and reads no view without it; then a refused session folder, a `view.json` that is missing, a link, a FIFO, empty, not JSON or unreadable, a view with no marker, and a marker whose workspace id or pane key fails its check are not a member. A session that is not a member gets no write, no log line and today's reply (W-C6, W§18 #89). `/zygo:down` reads its marker by the same rule (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/down.rs:136`). For a member, the verb takes `lock` with a 1 s bound; in the slash form the wait is also at most the time left in the 4.5 s hook bound (`verb_time_left`, `crates/zygo-hooks/src/prompt.rs:58`) less 500 ms for the reply, and with no time left the verb skips the write. It changes only a state that exists (`Store::update_existing`, `crates/zygo-workspace/src/state.rs:520`: it makes no folder, so a workspace that `ws forget` removed is not made again), checks that the record of the marker's pane key belongs to this zygo session by the rule of the hook writer (`record_of`, `workspace.rs:480`: its `pending` names the view's `session`, or its `zygo_session` is it), and writes `member.overrides` (the whole `overrides.json`, read under the lock by the session-file rule of `read_view`, `read_session_file` at `workspace.rs:94`: an `overrides.json` that is a link or not a regular file is not read, and a FIFO does not make the lock holder wait; `mirror`, `verbs.rs:217-261`) or `member.compact` (`set <w>`, where `<w>` is `off` or the token count of the change, or `cleared` for `on`; `zygo claude --compact off` parses as a set, `crates/zygo/src/cmd/claude.rs:30-35`). After a write, and only then, it runs the `snapshot.pending` check of W§7.3: the CLI form runs it inline; the slash form runs inside `zygo hook prompt`, so it takes the file and starts the snapshot as the detached `zygo ws snapshot` child, as the hooks do (`start_snapshot_child`, `workspace.rs:490`); when the zygo binary path cannot be read, it takes nothing. If the write fails or is skipped (no time left, the lock not taken within the bound, no workspace state, no record for the pane key, a record of another zygo session, a state or an `overrides.json` that cannot be read or written), the verb adds `; not saved for the workspace` at the end of its reply, or of its error text when it fails after the change (a lost PATCH, a failed `--save` write); its exit code does not change. Two setting verbs at the same time leave the record equal to the last `overrides.json`, because each mirror reads the file under the lock after its own write. Known limit: two `zygo compact` calls for one session at the same time can reach the proxy in one order and the record in the other; the record then holds the value that the proxy took first, until the next change. When a slash `down` or `workspace` does not finish within the hook bound, the reply says that it may still complete and that its shell form must not be run, because the shell form can name another workspace. The `compact` MCP tool changes no override: it only asks for one compaction (`crates/zygo-mcp/src/tools/compact.rs:22`; trigger 2 of §10.2.3), so it needs no write. | author |

## 7. State

### 7.1 Location and files

`paths.rs` derives every directory from `$HOME` on unix and does not read `XDG_*` variables (`crates/zygo-core/src/paths.rs:27-35`). The new directories follow that rule **(author)**:

| Path | Content |
|---|---|
| `$HOME/.local/state/zygo/workspaces/<id>/workspace.json` | The snapshot and member records (W§7.2). `0600`. |
| `…/<id>/lock` | Short write lock (fs4, as `session_dir::live`). |
| `…/<id>/op.lock` | Held by an `up` or `down` process for its whole life. A free lock while `phase` is `building`, `draining` or `stopping` means that process died. |
| `…/<id>/snapshot.pending` | A snapshot that must run later. A snapshot sets it when it cannot get `lock` within its wait, when its shrink reads stay unstable (AU89), or at its second `snapshot_seq` conflict (W§7.4 step 4). It holds `force` when a forced snapshot set it, else nothing (W§7.3). |
| `…/<id>/down.log` | Output of a detached `down` (AU97). |
| `$HOME/.local/state/zygo/down-all.log` | Output of a detached `down --all` (AU97). |
| `…/<id>/errors.log` | One line per failed hook or snapshot write. At most 64 KiB. |
| `…/<id>/mail/outbox/` | Messages to remote addresses that wait for a push; `refused/` holds the ones the secret pass refused, `tmp/` partial writes (mail sync design S§5, S§8.3 step 11). Folders `0700`, files `0600`. |
| `…/<id>/mail/sync/` | Mail sync state (mail sync design S§15): `identity` (S§7.5), `state.json` and `members.json` (S§8.3, S§7.3), `imported` and `incoming/` (S§8.5), `lock` and `again` (S§8.3), `last_start` (S§8.4). Folders `0700`, files `0600`. |
| `$HOME/.cache/zygo/workspaces/<id>/results/<pane key>/` | Results of a member, linked from each of its sessions (AU16). |
| `$HOME/.cache/zygo/workspaces/<id>/mail-sync/` | The detached worktree of the mail side branch (mail sync design S§6.3). Removed only by `zygo ws forget`, by path. |
| `$HOME/.cache/zygo/sessions/<ULID>/activity.json` | Turn and subagent state from the workspace hook (W§9.1). |
| `$HOME/.cache/zygo/sessions/<ULID>/activity.lock` | fs4 lock for changes to `activity.json` (AU56). |

New `Paths` methods (not fields, so no `Paths` literal changes): `state_dir()`, `workspaces_dir()` = `state_dir()/workspaces`, and `workspace_cache_dir()` = `cache_dir/workspaces`. `state_dir()` is `$HOME/.local/state/zygo` when `data_dir` is `$HOME/.local/share/zygo` (as `Paths::from_env` sets it on unix), and `data_dir/state` otherwise, so a `Paths` built by a test keeps its state under its own directory. On Windows these methods exist but no code uses them (OP5).

### 7.2 `workspace.json` fields

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `version` | `1` (AU29). Unknown fields are kept (AU78). |
| `id`, `name` | AU2; validated at read (AU84). |
| `common_dir`, `main_root` | Canonical paths at creation; `main_root` is the main worktree (AU77). |
| `tmux_socket` | The absolute tmux socket path (AU62). |
| `tmux_session` | `zygo-<name>`; validated at read (AU84). |
| `loadout` | The loadout that created the workspace or that `--reset` applied last. |
| `phase` | `building`, `up`, `draining`, `stopping` or `down`. |
| `phase_since` | Unix time. |
| `down_all` | `true` when a successful `down --all` stopped this workspace (AU19). |
| `snapshot_at` | Unix time of the last snapshot. |
| `snapshot_seq` | The count of snapshot writes; absent means 0. Each write of `windows[]` or `panes[]` adds 1. A snapshot that finds another value under `lock` than it read before tmux does not write (W§7.4 step 4). |
| `build_plan` | `loadout` or `restore`: the plan of the build that is not finished. `up` writes it with `phase = building` (W§8.1 step 8); the write that sets `phase = up` removes it. Absent when none; a file with no such field (an older zygo) reads as none (AU67). |
| `fresh_members` | The member keys that `up` step 8 created for that build (its fresh loadout members); written and removed with `build_plan`. Absent when empty. Each key is checked as a pane key (AU84). |
| `windows[]` | `index`, `name` (none when `automatic-rename` is on, AU55), `layout` (`#{window_layout}`), `width`, `height` (AU96), `active`, `zoomed`. |
| `panes[]` | One per pane, in window and pane-index order: `key`, `window`, `index`, `active`, `cwd` (`#{pane_current_path}`; an empty read, as for a dead pane, keeps the saved `cwd` of the same key, W§7.4 step 5), `kind` (`claude-code`, `opencode` or `shell`, from the member record, AU53). `up` writes an entry for each pane before it creates it (AU48). |
| `members{}` | By pane key. Fields below. |
| `member.zygo_session` | ULID of the last committed zygo session of this member. |
| `member.pending` | The start that is not committed yet (AU92), or none. Its `own_session` (absent means false, so an older file reads as before) marks an `agent_session` that is the user's own session argument (W§8.4). |
| `member.first_started_at` | Unix time (seconds) of the first committed start of this member record: the `pending.started_at` of the first commit (W AU92). Absent until then, and in a record of an older zygo (T§4). Never changed after it is set; a new record (a fresh loadout member, `up --reset`, a hand start that replaces the record, `zygo ws forget-pane`) starts without it. |
| `member.agent_session` | The agent session id, or none. A hook records any non-empty id that the payload carries; a payload with no id (missing, not a string, or empty) keeps the stored value, and the commit of `pending` (AU92) never replaces a stored id with none. For OpenCode the plugin sends only a known top session (AU27: a top session: its own `info` carried no `parentID` or a `null` one), so a child is never recorded. |
| `member.state` | `running`, `resumable` or `ended` (AU60). |
| `member.launch` | `kind`, `profile`, `job` (`orchestrator` or `worker`, set by `up` from the loadout pane and kept at each resume; absent for no job; workspace definition design R§9.3), `definition` (`{ commit, digest }`, the pin of the approved read of the workspace files; absent when the member has none; R§7.4), `passthrough` (also set by the pool, R§9.5), `server` (URL only, never the token, no user info, AU98), `compact` (the `--compact` text as launched), `yolo`, `config_dir`, `args` (filtered by AU30), `events_off` (`true` when the agent of this start sends no member events, from the arguments of the start before the filter. Claude Code: `--bare` by AU30's walk (a flag value and a token after `--` count as nothing), or `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` on, by Claude Code's rule, in the environment that its agent gets (AU26). OpenCode: `--pure`, the subcommand `attach`, or the subcommand `run` with `--attach`. The OpenCode subcommand is found as OpenCode 1.19.6 reads the tokens before it (`zygo_workspace::args::opencode_subcommand`, the one walk that `events_off` and the launcher's session arguments after `run` use; the flag matrix in `docs/work/phase2/nodes/FIX-24.md`, diff review round 3): `--` ends the flags, and there is no subcommand after it; the subcommand is the first token that does not start with `-` (or is `-`) and is not a flag value; a flag that holds `=` (`--x=v`, `-x=v`) takes no next token; a `--no-<x>` flag takes no next token; the global booleans of `fixtures/opencode/help.txt:34-38` (`-h`, `--help`, `-v`, `--version`, `--print-logs`, `--pure`) take the next token only when it is `true` or `false`; every other flag, long or short, in AU30's table or not, takes the next token unless it starts with `-` (also `--mini`, `--mdns`, `--auto`, `-c`, `--continue` and `--fork`, which help marks `[boolean]`: they are options of the default command, which the parser of the tokens before the subcommand does not know). `--pure` and `--attach` count when a token before `--` has that name before any `=` (a flag value never starts with `-`). AU30's filter uses the same value function (`opencode_takes_value`) for an OpenCode flag that its table does not hold, with the boolean options of every OpenCode command in place of the global booleans, because `ws run` restores the default command, which parses the tokens again with its own options (AU30). In a short cluster the function reads the last letter (`-xh` is read as `-h`). AU26, AU27 known limit 8. Every start write computes it again from the arguments of that start (AU92): the typed arguments of a hand start, also at an accepted AU94 resume, where the saved `args` stay; the saved `args` for a `ws run` start. `up` does not set it. Absent when `false`; a file with no such key reads as `false`. Known limit: an older zygo keeps the key as an unknown field and does not set it at its starts, so a member that an older zygo started (for example with `opencode attach <url>`) reads the value of the last start by a newer zygo, or `false` when there was none, until a newer zygo starts it again). Mirrors `Launch` at `crates/zygo/src/cmd/mod.rs:87-108`. |
| `member.config_dir`, `member.program` | The effective `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (absolute, or none) and the absolute agent program path, refreshed at each start (AU61, AU92). |
| `member.overrides` | The content of `overrides.json` (`crates/zygo-core/src/overrides.rs:10-19`) after its last change in a member session, or absent (OP12, AU105). |
| `member.compact` | `set <w>`, `cleared`, or absent (AU32, AU105). |
| `member.interrupted[]` | Subagents that `down --now` stopped: `agent_id` and `agent_type` only. |
| `member.closed_units[]` | Work unit ids that were open just before the stop (AU31). |
| `member.dropped_flags[]` | Names of content flags whose inline value AU30 dropped. Names only. |
| `member.clean_stop` | `true` when `down` saved this member (W§9.3 step 8); cleared by the AU75 repair and by a commit of a zygo session other than `saved_for` (AU60). |
| `member.note_pending` | Set by `up` for each member that it resumes; cleared by every hook commit of a pending start (the Claude Code first `SessionStart`, the OpenCode `session` call; AU60). The member wrapper's commit at agent exit (W§8.2 step 7) clears nothing, this field included (AU60 known limit). |
| `member.saved_for` | The zygo session (ULID) whose stop by `down` wrote `closed_units`, `interrupted` and `clean_stop` (W§9.3 step 8). Removed by the AU75 repair and by a commit of another zygo session (AU60). Absent when none. |

Size: a few KiB. `member.overrides` holds settings only (booleans, a byte threshold, guard levels), no secret. W-C7 holds: no field holds a token, a key, URL user info, prompt text, a subagent description or an inline flag value.

### 7.3 Writes and the lock

- Every write reads `workspace.json`, changes it, and writes it with `write_atomic` (W-C4). `write_atomic` (`crates/zygo-core/src/session_dir.rs:107-123`, now `pub(crate)`; make it public) creates a `tempfile::NamedTempFile` in the same directory, sets mode `0600`, syncs it, and renames it over the target with `persist`. On an error the temporary file is deleted when it is dropped. Only a process that dies between create and rename leaves a `.tmp*` file; the next writer deletes `.tmp*` files in the state directory older than 1 min.
- A reader reads at most 1 MiB + 1 byte of `workspace.json`; a larger file is refused (AU84). A state folder that is a symbolic link or not a folder is refused (`<path>: state folder is a link`, `<path>: state folder is not a folder`) before any create, mode change, lock or write. No file of a state folder (`workspace.json`, `lock`, `op.lock`, `errors.log`, `snapshot.pending`) is opened through a symbolic link, and none that is not a regular file is waited on. A symbolic link at `<state dir>/workspaces` or above it is followed: a user may link the data folder. A writer refuses a result larger than 1 MiB, the same limit, and writes nothing (`<path>: the new state is <n> bytes, more than the 1048576 bytes that every reader accepts (AU84); not written`); a hook logs that line once (AU56). Known limit: a recorded id that leaves `workspace.json` near the read limit can make a later write (an `up` that copies it, `down` step 8) exceed the limit; that write is refused and logged, and the stored state stays readable. Only hostile input makes such an id.
- A writer holds `lock` only to read, merge and write the file. It runs no tmux call and waits for no process while it holds `lock`, with one exception: the key assignment of W§7.4 step 4 (one `show-options` and at most one `set-option` per new pane, each with the 2 s bound). `up` and `down` run every tmux check without `lock` (W§8.1, W§9.3).
- Wait bounds: hooks, member events and their snapshots 1 s; snapshots run by `up` and `down` 5 s; launchers and the member wrapper 5 s; `up` and `down` 5 s, then exit 1 with `zygo: workspace <name> is busy; try again`.
- A snapshot that cannot get the lock within its wait (1 s for hooks and member events, 5 s for `up` and `down`) sets `snapshot.pending` and exits 0. A snapshot with `--force` writes `force` into it with `write_atomic`, which replaces any file in one rename; a snapshot without `--force` creates an empty file only when none exists (`create_new`) and never overwrites one, so no order of writers removes a `force`. Right after, the snapshot tries `lock` once more for at most 100 ms, without reading the state; when it gets it, it releases it and takes the file: no file means another writer took it; else it runs once more, with `--force` when either it or the file had `--force`. Each writer checks `snapshot.pending` after it releases `lock`. If the file exists, the writer takes it (one rename to `snapshot.pending.taken-<hex>`, a name that the `.tmp*` sweep never matches, so only one writer gets it and the sweep never deletes it) and runs one snapshot, with `--force` when the file held `force`. The taken name holds 128 random bits. A taker sets the time of `snapshot.pending` to now just before its rename, and reads and removes the taken file before it runs the snapshot, so a taken file older than 60 s was left by a process that died. Each take first handles such files: it renames each one to `snapshot.pending.recover-<128-bit hex>` (a name that neither this scan nor the `.tmp*` sweep matches), reads its flag, removes it, sets `snapshot.pending` again with that flag (a `force` file replaces; an empty one never overwrites) and writes one line to `errors.log`. A `snapshot.pending` that is not a regular file is not opened: it is removed, one line goes to `errors.log`, and it runs as a snapshot without `--force`. At most 64 bytes of the file are read. `up`, `down`, `zygo ws snapshot`, the member wrapper, a hand start (`assign_key`) and the CLI form of a verb run it inline; the slash form of a verb runs inside `zygo hook prompt`, so it starts it as the detached child below, as a hook process does (AU105). A hook process starts it as a detached child (`setsid`, stdin, stdout and stderr to `/dev/null`) and returns, so the hook does not wait for the 2 s check of AU89; when the child cannot start, the hook sets `snapshot.pending` again with the flag it took and writes one line to `errors.log`. A pending snapshot with `--force` also runs when `phase` is `stopping` (AU38), inline or in that child; `down` does not wait for it, and when it runs after the `kill-session` of W§9.3 step 11 it finds the session gone and writes nothing, so `up` restores the last written layout.
- `op.lock` serializes `up` and `down`. Each takes it at its start, with the 200 ms retry of AU83, and holds it for its whole life.
- Two kinds of writers change different fields. `ws snapshot` changes `windows[]` and `panes[]`. Launchers, the member wrapper and agent hooks change `members{}`. Both use the same lock.

### 7.4 Snapshot

Signature (hidden): `zygo ws snapshot --ws-id <id> [--event <hook name>] [--force]`.

- `--ws-id`: the workspace id (AU44, AU84). The socket is `tmux_socket` of `workspace.json` (AU62).
- `--event`: the tmux hook that started the snapshot, for `errors.log`; none for other triggers.
- `--force`: AU38.

Steps:

1. An invalid `--ws-id`: AU84 exception. Read `workspace.json` without the lock. No file: exit 0 and create nothing. A state that fails its checks (AU84) or is from a newer zygo (AU29): one line to `errors.log`, exit 0. If `phase` is `down` or `tmux_socket` is unset, exit 0. Without `--force`, also exit 0 when `phase` is `building` or `stopping`. Keep the `snapshot_seq` value read here. If `tmux_socket` is not the socket that the multiplexer of this snapshot is bound to (`Multiplexer::socket`), exit 0 with no tmux call. If `tmux_socket` fails AU54, write one line to `errors.log` (the AU54 text without `zygo: `) and stop with no write and no tmux call: `zygo ws snapshot` exits 0; a snapshot that the member wrapper, a verb, `up`, `down` or `zygo ws forget-pane` runs returns to its caller. This holds for every read of the state: the first one, the retry of step 4, the run after the 100 ms probe of W§7.3, and each `snapshot.pending` check.
2. Read the session option `@zygo-ws`. If it is set and is not the workspace id (AU63, AU84), or it is unset while `has-session` finds the session, write one line to `errors.log` and exit 0. Then run one `list-panes -s -t =<session> -F <format>` with window index, name, `automatic-rename`, layout, width, height, active and zoom flags, pane id, pane index, active flag, `@zygo-pane`, and the current path last (AU99). A pane that a linked window lists again is kept once, in its first window. Every tmux call has the 2 s bound. A tmux answer that the session or server is gone (AU87), from this or any later tmux call of the snapshot: exit 0 with no write and no log line. Other tmux error, a line that does not parse, no line at all, a `@zygo-pane` that is set and not valid (AU84), or one key on two panes: one line to `errors.log`, exit 0.
3. Shrink rule (AU89): if a key that `panes[]` holds is missing from the read, wait 2 s, do the `@zygo-ws` check of step 2 again, and read again, checked as in step 2, until two reads in a row show the same pane ids (at most four reads; else set `snapshot.pending` and exit 0). Use the last read.
4. Take `lock` (AU41) within the wait of W§7.3. If `phase` is now `down`, or `building` or `stopping` without `--force`, or `tmux_session` changed, or `tmux_socket` is not the socket of the multiplexer, release and exit 0 with no tmux call. If `snapshot_seq` is not the value of step 1, another snapshot wrote after this one read: release and do the whole snapshot again from step 1, once; a second time, set `snapshot.pending` and exit 0. For each pane that had no `@zygo-pane`, read `@zygo-pane` again. A valid key: use it. If another pane of this read holds that key, one line to `errors.log`, exit 0 with no write. A value that is not valid: one line to `errors.log`, exit 0 with no write. Still empty: make a new key that no `panes[]` entry, no member record and no pane of this read holds, set the tmux option, and use the key. A pane that closed meanwhile, or any other error of the pane option read or set (except a gone answer, which exits 0 at once): release; if `has-session` finds the session gone, exit 0; else one line to `errors.log` and exit 0, with no write in both cases. `assign_key` changes only a state that exists (`Store::update_existing`): when `ws forget` removed the state after the read, it sets no key and makes no folder (`no workspace state`).
5. Under the same lock: replace `windows[]` and `panes[]` by the read, set `snapshot_at`, and add 1 to `snapshot_seq`; a window keeps the unknown fields of the stored window with the same index only when one of that stored window's panes has a key that is on a pane of the window now (a new window at the index of a closed one keeps none), and a pane keeps those of the stored pane with the same key (AU78); a pane whose read path is empty (tmux gives no `#{pane_current_path}` for a dead pane) keeps the `cwd` of the stored pane with the same key, else its `cwd` is empty; a window with `automatic-rename` on gets no name; each pane's `kind` comes from its member record (AU53). Do not delete member records. Write. Release. A write that fails (the state cannot be read again, fails its checks, or cannot be replaced) writes one line to `errors.log`. Then do the `snapshot.pending` check of W§7.3.

Triggers **(author)**:

- tmux hooks on the workspace session only (W-C3): `after-new-window`, `after-split-window`, `window-layout-changed`, `window-renamed`, `client-detached`, and `after-kill-pane`, `pane-exited`, `window-unlinked` (a `kill-window` fires no `after-kill-pane`). No focus hooks (AU90). Each runs the fixed string of AU54, which snapshots the whole session, so the window that a hook runs for does not change the result. `window-unlinked` is set with `set-hook -w` on each window; tmux 3.3 and later store it on the session (PW6 6.30). Reliability (PW6, 2026-11-22; node E1 probes 2026-11-23 on tmux 3.2a, 3.4, 3.7c and 3.8: `fixtures/tmux/e1-hooks-<version>.txt` and `fixtures/tmux/e1-active-pane-<version>.txt`, scripts in `fixtures/tmux/probes/`): on tmux 3.7c and earlier (probed: 3.2a, 3.4, 3.7c), when the target of an event is gone at the time its hook runs, tmux runs the hook of the session that it takes as current: the session with a client, else the most recent one. This applies to `after-kill-pane`, `pane-exited` and `window-unlinked`, and to `window-layout-changed` when the closed pane was the active pane of its window. So these fire for the workspace only when it is that session, and then they also fire for panes of other sessions (a snapshot that changes nothing). tmux 3.8, the release after 3.7c, runs the workspace hook of `pane-exited`, `window-unlinked` and `window-layout-changed` in these cases too (`e1-hooks-3.8.txt` cases B, D, E, F; `e1-active-pane-3.8.txt`); `after-kill-pane` still runs only when the workspace is the current session (case C on all four versions). A later version is taken to behave as 3.8. A pane that is not the active pane of its window, closed in a window that keeps other panes, always fires `window-layout-changed` on that window (cases A and F, and `kill-pane on pane 1`, on all four versions). In the probes, a `window-renamed` of that window also ran for the workspace after each close of a pane that ran `sh`, also when the window name stayed the same, and after no close of a pane that ran `sleep` (`e1-active-pane-<version>.txt`; `e1-hooks-<version>.txt` cases A, B, E). An agent pane closed by hand is always recorded by the member wrapper at agent exit.
- The member wrapper at agent start and at agent exit.
- The `SessionStart` hook, and for OpenCode the plugin's `session` call, when the agent session id changes; the hook starts the snapshot as the detached child of W§7.3, one child with the pending check.
- `down`, before it stops anything (final snapshot, with `--force`).
- `up`, at the end of the build (with `--force`).

Known limit: a `cd` in a shell pane changes no tmux state, so the saved cwd is the cwd at the last trigger. `down` always takes a final snapshot. After a crash, a shell pane can come back in an older cwd. Agent panes do not change cwd.

Known limit (PW6, tmux 3.7c and earlier; probed 3.2a, 3.4 and 3.7c): while the workspace session is not tmux's current session, these closes may not be written until the next trigger: a whole window of shell panes that closes (`kill-window`, or its last shell exits), and a shell pane that closes while it is the active pane of a window that keeps other panes (`kill-pane`, or its shell exits). For the pane, no `window-layout-changed`, `after-kill-pane` or `pane-exited` runs for the workspace; a `window-renamed` from automatic-rename can write it sooner: in the probes a `window-renamed` of that window ran after each close of a pane that ran `sh`, also when the window name did not change, and none ran after the close of a pane that ran `sleep` (`fixtures/tmux/e1-active-pane-<version>.txt`; `fixtures/tmux/e1-hooks-<version>.txt` cases B and E). For the window, no hook ran for the workspace (`e1-hooks-<version>.txt` case D). A crash before the write brings the window or pane back at `up` as shells in their saved cwd. tmux 3.8 runs the workspace's `window-unlinked` hook for the window and its `window-layout-changed` hook for the pane, so both are written at once (`e1-hooks-3.8.txt` case D; `e1-active-pane-3.8.txt`, `kill-pane on pane 0`).

Known limit: `up` sets the window-scoped hooks (`window-layout-changed`, `window-renamed`, `pane-exited`, `window-unlinked`) and `remain-on-exit off` on each window that it builds. A window that the operator creates later has none of them; its changes are written at the next session hook (`after-new-window`, `after-split-window`, `client-detached`, `after-kill-pane`) or member wrapper snapshot.

### 7.5 Panes closed by hand (AU35)

1. A pane closed while the session lives (`kill-pane`, `kill-window`, an exit of `ws run --shell`'s shell) leaves the layout at the next snapshot, after the 2 s check (AU89). If it held a member record, that member is **detached**: its record stays, and `up` does not recreate it. The snapshot comes from `window-layout-changed`, from the member wrapper at agent exit, or from the W§7.4 hooks when the workspace session is tmux's current session (on tmux 3.8, also when it is not). The known limit of W§7.4 applies to shell panes on tmux 3.7c and earlier.
2. Any other loss of panes (a teardown, a server death, a reboot) writes nothing: a teardown's shrinking snapshots find the session gone after 2 s (AU89), and a crash fires no hook. The members stay `resumable` (AU17), and `up` restores the last written layout.
3. For each detached member with an `agent_session` and state `resumable`, `up` prints `zygo: <kind> agent session <id> is detached (its pane was closed); resume it by hand or run zygo ws forget-pane <key>`. `zygo ws forget-pane <key>` or `up --reset` deletes the record.

## 8. `zygo up`

### 8.1 Sequence

1. On Windows: exit 2, `workspaces need tmux; run zygo in WSL` (OP5).
1a. The update check (auto-update design), after step 1 and before any library call. It runs one time for each `up`, also with `--all`, which asks one time for all its workspaces. On `y` with a successful install, the check prints `updated to <new>; relaunch zygo`, and `up` exits 0 and starts nothing. If the install fails, the check prints its line, and `up` exits with its code and starts nothing. Every other result continues with step 2.
2. Find `tmux` on `PATH` and read `tmux -V`. No tmux: exit 2, `workspaces need tmux; install tmux 3.2 or later`. Older version: exit 2 with the found version.
3. Find the workspace: by `<name>` from the state directory (AU20), else from the cwd with `zygo_work::repo::checkout` (`repo.rs:137`) and the main worktree (AU77). Not a repository: exit 2 (AU25). A state that does not parse, or fails AU84: exit 1 naming the file (`--reset` renames it to `workspace.json.bad`). A state file larger than 1 MiB is refused, not invalid (AU84): `up` exits 1 with or without `--reset`, and the file is never renamed. By `<name>`, the readable states are matched first. Then a state that cannot be read matches when the `name` string of its JSON is `<name>`: a newer state by that alone, an invalid state only when that name passes AU2, `common_dir` and `main_root` are non-empty absolute paths, and its folder is the W§5 id of `common_dir`. One match: a newer state exits 1 with the AU29 text, an invalid one follows the rule above. Two or more: exit 1 with `zygo: more than one workspace state has the name <name>: <file>, <file>`. In the own folder of a bare repository, where `checkout` finds no work tree, `up` reads `git worktree list --porcelain` there: with a non-bare entry it takes the checkout of that main worktree; with none it exits 2 with the AU77 text.
4. Compute `id` (AU2). Check the zygo path by AU54 (exit 2 before anything changes). Take `op.lock` (AU83 retry); held: exit 1, `zygo: up or down is in progress for <name>`.
5. Repair a dead `up` or `down` (no lock needed for the read; writes under `lock`), on the server of step 6; before any use of a saved `tmux_socket` (the AU62 check included), in every phase, check it by AU54 and exit 2 on a failure. A repair error that is the AU54 refusal of `repair` exits 2 with the AU54 line once; any other repair error exits 1 with `zygo: <error>`. `phase = building` → AU67 (by `build_plan` when the session is gone), only on a session whose `@zygo-ws` is the id (a session without it or with another value: exit 1, AU63; it is never adopted), after the session options and hooks of step 10 are set again; when `tmux_socket` is unset (an `up` that died before its step 9 write), the repair reads the socket with `display-message -p -t =zygo-<name> '#{socket_path}'`, checks it by AU54 and AU62, and writes it with `phase = up`; `phase = draining` → `phase = up` if the session exists, else `down`; `phase = stopping` → AU75. `repair` runs no `snapshot.pending` check after its writes; `up` and `down` call it right after `repair` returns (W§7.3). A state that does not parse or fails AU84 is not repaired: without `--reset` `up` exits 1 (W§10); with `--reset` it is renamed to `workspace.json.bad`, with no check of its socket, after the plan of step 7 passed all its checks and before the write of step 8. The repair of this step is the only write of `up` before those checks. Each repair write that sets `phase = up` removes `build_plan` and `fresh_members`.
6. Check the tmux session `zygo-<name>` on `tmux_socket` (AU62), without `lock`; when no socket is saved, on the server of `$TMUX`, whose socket gets the AU54 check before this call (exit 2). If it exists and `@zygo-ws` is the id (AU63): with `--reset` or `--loadout`, exit 1 (AU37); else set `remain-on-exit off` on each window of the session that holds a pane with a valid `@zygo-pane`, best effort (AU68; a user window is not changed): a gone session or window prints nothing, a failed read of the windows prints `zygo: <name>: could not release the build hold: <error>`, and a failure on one window prints `zygo: <name>: could not release the build hold of window <window>: <error>` and the next window is tried; then, when step 5 repaired it from `building`, run one snapshot with `--force` and then the `snapshot.pending` check, with the state's `tmux_socket` (as in step 12); then attach (step 13) and stop. If it exists with another `@zygo-ws`: exit 1 (AU63). If the answer is not "gone" by AU87 (an unreachable session with a live launcher, or another error): exit 1.
7. Choose the plan:
   - `--reset`, no `workspace.json`, or a state with no pane: the loadout: `--loadout`; else `[workspace] default_loadout` when the user config file sets it; else `repository` when the default branch has `.zygo/workspace.toml`; else `solo` (workspace definition design R§10, `zygo up` row). A `repository` plan comes from one checked read of the default branch (R§7.3) and gives each pane the pin (R§7.4); a read that is not approved, invalid, unread or absent, and a user `[loadout.repository]`, are as that row. Then the member maximum (R§9.4, the repository loadout only) and the pool checks (R§9.5), all before step 8. With `--reset`, first print one line per member in state `running` or `resumable` with an agent session id (AU37). Members start fresh. A loadout pane whose `server` holds user info becomes a shell pane with no member record, and `up` prints the AU98 line.
   - Else: the saved `windows[]` and `panes[]`, windows in index order and panes in index order. A member in state `running` or `resumable`, with an `agent_session`, with its pane key in `panes[]`, and whose launcher is not live (AU87), resumes. Every other pane is a shell (OP8). A member in state `running` or `resumable` without an `agent_session` gives the W§10 line. `--loadout` without `--reset` is ignored with one line.
   - Every window name passes the control-character check and every saved size is in 1..=10000, else exit 1 before step 8 (AU96). No value that goes into a tmux command line may end with `;`, which tmux reads as the end of a command: the main worktree (`-c`), each window name and layout (from the loadout or the saved state) and each item of each pane's argument list (AU15). Such a value makes `up` exit 1 with `zygo: <what> "<value>" ends with `;`, which tmux reads as the end of a command; nothing was written`, before any write of this run except the repair of step 5 and before the `workspace.json.bad` rename; when that repair wrote, the text ends with `nothing of this plan was written`.
8. Take `lock`. Write the plan: a full `panes[]` entry for every pane (AU48), member records (for fresh loadout members: `launch` from the loadout pane, AU45), `phase = building`, `build_plan` (`loadout` or `restore`) and `fresh_members` (the keys of the fresh loadout members; empty for a restore); when `tmux_socket` is unset and step 9 will use a known server (the socket of `$TMUX`, after its AU62 check), set `tmux_socket` to it, so a later `up` and its repair ask the same server; for each resumed member, `note_pending = true`; delete the member record of each pane restored as a shell when that record, as read under `lock`, has no agent session id (`agent_session` or `pending.agent_session`) and no pending start (AU53), and keep a whole copy of it for the undo of step 9. Release `lock`. Whether this write set `tmux_socket` is decided in this write.
9. Create the session detached with the **first pane of the first window**, and tag it in the same tmux invocation: `new-session -d -s zygo-<name> -c <main worktree, # doubled> -x <width> -y <height> -- <ws run argument list> ; set-option -t =zygo-<name>: @zygo-ws <id> ; set-option -p -t =zygo-<name>: @zygo-pane <key> ; set-option -t =zygo-<name>: destroy-unattached off ; set-option -w -t =zygo-<name>: remain-on-exit on` (AU55, AU68, AU96), with the environment of AU64 and `-e ZYGO_WORKSPACE=<id>` (session scope only). Never put it in the client environment: a server that `new-session` starts copies the client environment into its global environment, and every other session of that server would then get the id. So no session of this workspace exists without `@zygo-ws`, and a global `destroy-unattached on`, which destroys a detached session when its command list ends (tmux 3.2a and 3.7c), does not end it. The `remain-on-exit on` of the first window is the build hold: tmux runs the items of one invocation in one command-queue pass, before it handles the exit of the new pane (tmux 3.2a `cmd-queue.c`; checked live on tmux 3.2a and 3.8), so a first pane whose command exits at once stays as a dead pane, and the session stays until step 12 ends the hold. Then `resize-window -t =zygo-<name>:<first window> -x <width> -y <height>` (AU96), and, when the first window has a saved or loadout name, `rename-window -t =zygo-<name>:<first window> -- <name>` with `#` doubled (AU55, AU99; `new-session` takes no name, so the first window is renamed as `new-window` renames the others). The server: the server of `$TMUX` when set, else tmux's default resolution; a restore uses `tmux_socket` (AU62). Read the socket path with `display-message -p -t =zygo-<name> '#{socket_path}'` and check it by AU54 and AU62. On a failed check: `kill-session`, then the undo, then exit 2 (or 1 for AU62). When `new-session` fails, ask the same server for `zygo-<name>` and its `@zygo-ws`: the id → `phase` stays `building`, print the error and exit 1 (the next `up` repairs it, AU67); no session → the undo, exit 1; another value or none → print the error, the undo, then exit 1 with AU63; an answer that tells neither → `phase` stays `building`, exit 1. The undo is one write under `lock`: `phase = down`; then by `build_plan` as AU67 does when the session is gone; each record that step 8 deleted is put back, whole; a `tmux_socket` that the step 8 write set is removed; `build_plan` and `fresh_members` are removed. After the write, the `snapshot.pending` check with the `tmux_socket` of the state written. A failed undo write prints `zygo: state not cleaned: <error>`. Else write `tmux_socket` under `lock`.
10. On this session only: `set-environment -r` for each name of AU64, `set-environment ZYGO_WORKSPACE <id>`, user option `@zygo-ws <id>`, `destroy-unattached off` (AU68), the hooks of W§7.4 (AU54). The first window keeps `remain-on-exit on`, the build hold of step 9, until step 12.
11. For the other panes of the first window, `split-window`. For each later window, set `default-size` (AU96), then `new-window -a -t =zygo-<name>:{end}` (with `-c` as in step 9) with its first pane and, in the same invocation, `; set-option -w -t =zygo-<name>:{end} remain-on-exit on`: the build hold of step 9 for this window, which `-a` after `{end}` makes the window with the highest index. Then `rename-window` with `#` doubled when it has a saved name (AU99), then `split-window` for the rest; a split pane has the hold of its window (`remain-on-exit` is a window and pane option, tmux 3.2a `options-table.c`). Known limit: a user's global `after-new-window` hook runs before the chained `set-option`; when it creates a window, `{end}` names that window, so the user's window can get `remain-on-exit on` and the new zygo window is not held; neither the release of step 12 nor the release of step 6 sets that window back to `off` (it holds no pane with a `@zygo-pane`, AU68); and when it makes the command queue wait (a `run-shell` without `-b`), tmux can handle a pane exit first. zygo adds no code for these cases. Each pane gets its key (`@zygo-pane`) and its start command: the `ws run` argument list of AU15, with `--shell` for a shell pane (AU46). A failed split: AU96. Then `select-layout <layout>` per window. A layout that tmux refuses (wrong pane count) falls back to `tiled` with one warning line. Agent panes that start now write their member records under `lock` (W§7.3); they do not wait for `up`. Step 11 ends with `set-option -w -u -t =zygo-<name>:<first window> window-size` (AU96), also when it builds no pane.
12. Select the saved active window and pane; restore zoom. Wait at most 2 s, reading `list-panes` each 50 ms, until each built pane whose planned cwd is an existing folder reports that folder as `#{pane_current_path}`; a dead pane reports no folder, so with a dead pane the wait runs its full 2 s, which is bounded and ends no user work. Then run one snapshot with `--force` (AU38) in every case; it runs during the build hold, so it records every pane. The wait keeps the snapshot from recording the start folder of a `ws run` that has not changed to its cwd yet. The snapshot merges the member records written in step 11. Then end the build hold, best effort for each window: `set-option -w -t =zygo-<name>:<window> remain-on-exit off` on each built window (AU68); a window that is gone prints nothing, and any other error prints `zygo: <name>: could not release the build hold of window <window>: <error>` and the next window is tried. A pane whose command exited while held stays as a dead pane, with its key, its `panes[]` entry and its saved cwd. Read the panes of the session (a gone session prints nothing; another error prints `zygo: <name>: could not read the dead panes: <error>`, and no pane is read), then `#{pane_dead}` and `#{pane_dead_status}` of each pane with a pane key, best effort for each pane (a gone pane prints nothing; another error prints `zygo: <name>: could not read pane <key>: <error>`, and the next pane is read). Take `lock`; set `phase = up`, `down_all = false`; write; release. A stop of `up` before this write, also during the hold, leaves `phase = building` for the repair of AU67. Print the permission flags of each resumed member (AU70), one warning per dropped flag (AU30), one line per detached member (W§7.5) and per pane not built (AU96), and one line per dead pane: `zygo: pane <key> exited during up (exit <n>); its dead pane stays: tmux -S '<socket>' respawn-pane -t <pane id> starts it again` (`no exit status` in place of `exit <n>` when a signal ended it; the socket is quoted as in AU100). Release `op.lock`. `up` never waits for a dead pane, and a dead pane changes no exit code: `up` exits 0 without attach, and with the code of step 13 with attach. Known limit: a pane that exits after the hold ends closes as in W§7.5; when it was the last pane, the session ends before the `phase = up` write, the write still sets `phase = up`, and the attach of step 13 fails with tmux's error. The next `up` (or `up --all`, AU52) finds the session gone and no live launcher, and restores the layout that the final snapshot saved (steps 6-7).
13. Attach: with `--no-attach` or `ZYGO_SESSION` set (AU95), print `zygo: <name> is up` and exit 0. Inside tmux on another server: AU100. Outside tmux, `attach-session -t =zygo-<name>`; inside tmux on the same server, `switch-client -t =zygo-<name>`.

`up --all` takes no `<name>`, `--loadout` or `--reset` (exit 2). It runs the tmux check of step 2 first (exit 2 when tmux is missing or older than 3.2), before any session check. It runs steps 3-12 for each workspace with `down_all = true`, and for each workspace left with `phase = up` and no tmux session (AU52, W-G3), one after the other in name order. The session check runs the AU54 check of the saved socket and then the AU62 check of the socket first. A workspace whose session exists is not restored and gets no line; when that session holds this workspace's `@zygo-ws`, `up --all` takes `op.lock` (held: it changes nothing), checks the session and its `@zygo-ws` again, and ends a left build hold as in step 6 (AU68); then, when `down_all = true`, it clears `down_all` under `lock`, because the workspace is up, and runs the `snapshot.pending` check of W§7.3. First it reports each state that does not parse, fails AU84 or is newer: `zygo: workspace <id> skipped: <error>` (AU80). Then it prints one line per chosen workspace: `zygo: <name> is up` on stdout, or, after the lines of that `up`, `zygo: <name> skipped` on stderr; a workspace whose `op.lock` is held, whose `up` fails, whose saved socket fails AU54 (the skip line gives the reason, AU54), whose socket fails AU62, or whose session is unreachable (AU87) is skipped. When no workspace reaches its restore (there is no candidate, or each candidate runs or is skipped before its `up`), it prints `zygo: up --all found no workspace to restore`. It attaches to none and exits 1 when it skipped one.

### 8.2 `zygo ws run` (one pane)

Signature (hidden): `zygo ws run --ws-id <id> --pane <key> [--shell]`.

1. Validate the id and the key (AU84). Run step 1a. Clean the own environment (AU64). Read the pane's entry in `panes[]` by pane key, and its member record (no lock needed for a read). Take the cwd from the `panes[]` entry (AU48) and change to it (AU55). If the cwd does not exist: change to `main_root`; for an agent pane, do not resume (AU61): print the AU61 line and go to step 8; for a shell pane, print `zygo: <cwd> is gone`. With `--shell`, go to step 8.
1a. The update check (auto-update design), before the environment is cleaned, so it sees `ZYGO_WORKSPACE`. The check does not run when `ZYGO_RELAUNCHED` is set. `ws run` reads the canonical path of its own binary before the check. On `y` with a successful install, the check prints `updated to <new>; relaunch zygo`. `ws run` then executes that path with the same arguments, the same environment and `ZYGO_RELAUNCHED=1`. The new process does not run the check, also when the check could not write its cache, and continues with the rest of step 1. If the relaunch fails, print `zygo: cannot relaunch zygo ws run: <error>` and continue with the rest of step 1 as a normal `ws run`, so the member starts. If the install fails, the check prints its line and `ws run` continues with the rest of step 1.
2. Build `LaunchOpts` (`crates/zygo-launch/src/launcher.rs:97-128`) from `member.launch` through `launch_opts` (AU45), with the member fields (W§8.4), at a resume, the `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` rule and the program rule of AU61, and `LaunchOpts.member = Some(Member { ws_id, pane })` (AU102), also when `member.launch.args` hold `--settings` (AU59). Compaction: AU32.
3. Add the session argument: resume: Claude Code `--resume=<id>`, OpenCode `--session=<id>`, one token each (AU3); fresh Claude Code: `--session-id=<new UUID v4>` (W§8.3).
4. Set `LaunchOpts.resume_note` from the member record when `note_pending = true` (AU72), with `agent_session` = the id that step 3 resumes, none for a fresh start (W§9.4). Set the launcher callback (AU34). It first writes the member record (AU92) with `Store::update_existing`, which changes only a state that still exists and only the record of the pane key; it makes no record. In the same locked write, before the start fields, it applies the live-launcher check of the hand start (W§8.4): when the record's `zygo_session` or `pending.zygo_session` names a live launcher, the record does not change, the callback prints `zygo: pane <key> holds session <id>, whose launcher is still running; zygo starts no second agent on it` and returns `StopLaunch` (AU34), so no agent starts and the pane gets its shell (step 8) (W-C9). When the state or the record is gone (`zygo ws forget` or `zygo ws forget-pane` ran after step 1), the callback makes nothing more: no results folder, no `overrides.json`, no snapshot. The launch goes on with the member marker of step 2 in its view and environment and no record, so the hooks and the verb mirror find no record and change nothing (AU105). When the write fails for another reason (`lock` not taken within the 5 s bound, an I/O error, a state that cannot be read), the callback also makes nothing more, and the launch goes on (C6, `launcher.rs:406-411`): the agent runs with no record of this start, so no live check (`up`, `ws forget-pane`, the hand-start refusal of AU94) sees it. The callback prints `zygo: could not record the launch of pane <key>: <error>; zygo cannot see this agent as live` on the pane's stderr, and the launcher writes the error to the session log. After the write, the callback makes the `results` symlink (AU16). When `member.overrides` is present, it writes it into the new `overrides.json`, and, when `redaction_content` is set, sends it with `PATCH /v1/zygo/sessions/{id}/settings` (the proxy session already exists when the callback runs, `launcher.rs:378`; hook-side settings are read from `overrides.json` on each hook call, §17.1). There is no `clean_stop` gate (OP12).
5. Keep the handling of SIGHUP, SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGQUIT as it is; the launcher's handler catches the four while it runs (AU88).
6. Run the launcher in process (`Launcher::run`, `launcher.rs:195`).
7. With `spawned = false`, delete `member.pending` when it names this start; the member stays as it was (AU60, AU92), and `ws run` prints `zygo: the <kind> launch failed before the agent started (exit <n>)`. Else commit the pending start if no hook has done it (AU92; never an `agent_session` marked `own_session`, W§8.4), and classify (AU17, AU73). A resume **failed fast** when the launcher received no signal, no signal ended the agent, the code is non-zero, and the agent ran less than 10 s from its spawn (`LaunchOutcome.ran`); then the member stays `resumable` and `ws run` prints `zygo: resume of <kind> session <id> failed (exit <n>); try: <resume command>`. Write the member record under `lock` with `Store::update_existing`, only when the callback wrote the start: it changes only a record of this start (its `zygo_session` or its `pending` names this zygo session) in a state that still exists, so after `zygo ws forget` or `zygo ws forget-pane` it makes and changes nothing. When the lock is not taken within its bound or the write fails, `ws run` prints `zygo: could not save the exit record of pane <key>: <error>`, prints no line that needs the record, and keeps the agent's exit code. `<kind>` is `claude` or `opencode`; the resume command is the AU61 command of the member record as the exit write left it, else as the start write left it: `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<dir> zygo claude<flags> -- <args>` for Claude Code when the launch has a config dir, else `env -u CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR zygo claude<flags> -- <args>`, and `zygo opencode<flags> -- <args>` for OpenCode (AU3, AU61), each path, value, argument and the id one shell word (AU61).
8. Set `ZYGO_WORKSPACE=<id>` (AU93). Set the default handling of SIGHUP, SIGTERM, SIGINT and SIGQUIT (AU88); `exec $SHELL` (else `/bin/sh`) in the cwd.

Steps 4, 5 and 7 are the **member wrapper**. A hand-started member (AU9) runs the same wrapper from `zygo claude` or `zygo opencode`, without step 8; it does not change signal handling (AU88).

### 8.3 Session-id pre-assign

For Claude Code, when the arguments (after AU30 filtering, or as typed for a hand-started member) hold none of `--session-id`, `-r`/`--resume`, `-c`/`--continue`, `--fork-session`, `--from-pr`, `--cloud`, `--teleport`, `--remote` (the session rows of the AU30 table in `crates/zygo-workspace/src/args.rs`, read by the same parser, so a token after `--` or a flag value is not a session argument), the member wrapper passes `LaunchOpts.agent_session = Some(AgentSession::Preassign(<new UUID v4>))`, the launcher adds `--session-id=<new UUID v4>` (one token) before the user's arguments, so before any `--`, and the wrapper records the id in `member.pending` (AU3, AU9, AU92). With a session argument of the user's own, the id comes only from the `SessionStart` hook. OpenCode has no pre-assign; its id comes from the plugin (PW4).

### 8.4 Membership

A launch is a member when all these are true:

- `ZYGO_SESSION` is not set, with any value, the empty one included (not a launch inside an agent's tool).
- `TMUX` is set and not empty, and `$TMUX_PANE` is a tmux pane id (`%` and 1 to 10 digits). Otherwise the launch is not in tmux: a v1 launch with no tmux call and no workspace line.
- `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` is a valid workspace id (AU84).
- The pane is a workspace pane: on the server of `$TMUX` (`-S` with its socket), `display -p -t $TMUX_PANE '#{session_name} #{@zygo-ws}'` gives `zygo-<name>` and the id of `ZYGO_WORKSPACE`. Before that call, the state of that id is read: when it exists, its `tmux_socket` must pass AU54 and be the socket of `$TMUX`; then the socket of `$TMUX` gets the AU54 check and the AU62 check.
- The server URL holds no user info (AU98). A user `--settings` does not change membership (AU59).
- The state exists and can be read, names this tmux session, and is in phase `up` or `draining`; the pane gets its key (AU79).
- AU94 and W-C9 do not refuse.

These checks are for a hand start only; `ws run` passes `LaunchOpts.member` explicitly (AU102). The checks run in the order above. A failure before the pane is known as a workspace pane is a silent v1 launch, except a state whose `tmux_socket` fails AU54 or is not the socket of `$TMUX`, or a socket of `$TMUX` that fails AU54 or AU62. The user-info check prints its AU98 line only in a workspace pane, and before any key or record is written. Every later failure prints `zygo: this agent is not saved by the workspace: <reason>` and goes on as a v1 launch: `no workspace state for <id>`; the state read error (`<path>: <reason>`, `<path>: state folder is a link`, …); `the workspace state names tmux session <a>, not <b>`; the AU54 text without its `zygo: ` (no socket is printed); `the workspace state names tmux server <a>, not <b>` (`<a>` is `none` when unset); the AU62 text; the key error of `assign_key` (`cannot assign a key to pane <pane>: <error>`, for example `… a member starts only in phase up or draining, not down`); and, in the session callback, the reasons below. Paths and ids are escaped (C6).

A hand start writes nothing to the member record before the launch; it only gets the pane key (W§7.4 step 4, AU79). The record is written by the launcher callback (AU34), under `lock` with the 5 s bound of launchers, in one write with the start fields: state `running`, the agent kind, `launch`, `pending` (with this zygo session and, for a fresh Claude Code start, the pre-assigned id; for a start with an own session argument, that argument, marked `own_session`, W-C9 and AU92), `config_dir` and `program` (W§8.2 step 4). The accepted resume of AU94 keeps the saved `launch` and `dropped_flags` of the record; only `launch.events_off` is computed again, from the arguments typed for this start (W§7.2). In the same lock the callback checks the phase, AU94 and W-C9 again, because the state can change during the launch.
