When a check refuses, or the write fails, or the state is gone, the callback writes nothing, prints `zygo: this agent is not saved by the workspace: <reason>` (`a member starts only in phase up or draining, not <phase>`; `pane <key> holds resumable session <id>`; `pane <key> holds session <id>, whose launcher is still running`; `agent session <id> belongs to pane <other key>`; `no workspace state for <id>`; `could not write the member record: <error>`), and the launch goes on as a v1 launch: the callback returns `NotMember`, so the launcher drops the member before it writes the view, the settings and the agent environment (AU34), and removes from the agent arguments the session argument that it added for the pre-assign (`--session-id=<id>`, one token); the arguments that the user typed stay as typed. A launch that ends before the callback leaves the record of the key as it was. A launch whose callback wrote the record but whose agent was not spawned (`spawned = false`) puts back, in the exit write, the record that the key had before the callback (or no record when it had none), only when the record is still exactly the record that the callback wrote; when another write changed the record since, the exit write removes only the `pending` of this start and keeps the rest; any other record is not changed. A hand start prints the exit lines of W§8.2 step 7 only when its callback wrote the member record; a v1 launch, a launch that the callback made a v1 launch, and a launch that ends before the callback print none. The resume command of those lines is made from the member record (W§8.2 step 7), so after an accepted resume it names the saved launch, not the flags typed for the resume.

A member launcher sets `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` and `ZYGO_PANE` in the child environment (§8.4 "Set"), registers the member hooks (W§9.1), writes the membership marker into the session view (AU58), and runs the member wrapper (W§8.2). For every launch that is not a member, the launcher removes `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` and `ZYGO_PANE` from the child environment (AU58). Such a launch is a v1 launch in every other way (OP9, W-C6).

## 9. `zygo down` and the drain

### 9.1 Hooks for members

Claude Code, registered only for members, in `ClaudeCode::hooks` (`crates/zygo-launch/src/claude_code.rs`) **(author)**. "Main loop" means an input without an `agent_id` key (the test of §8.3).

| Event | Matcher | Command | Effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| `SessionStart` | — | existing `session-start`, for members as `session-start --event member` | Main loop: prints the resume note once (W§9.4); then, at the first `SessionStart` of the zygo session (its member record's `pending` names this session), commits `member.pending` (AU92) and clears `note_pending`, and clears `closed_units`, `interrupted`, `clean_stop` and `saved_for` only when `saved_for` is not this session (AU60); when the id changed, writes `member.agent_session` and starts a snapshot (W§7.4). A member's session view that cannot be read gives one session-log line and no output (AU84). A member's session folder that is a link or a relative path is checked first: one stderr line, the session text with no note, and no write into the folder. A rewrite of the session view keeps the top-level fields this zygo does not know. |
| `UserPromptSubmit` | — | existing `prompt`; for members also `workspace --event prompt` | `prompt`: a `/zygo:` line runs as a verb (§17.1). A launch that is not a member keeps its v1 behaviour: `prompt` reads no session view and nothing else in the session folder, and a verb that panics lets the line pass to the model (C6: one stderr line, exit 0). For a member (`ZYGO_WORKSPACE` set), a verb that panics also lets the line pass, and `prompt` also marks the member busy: the `prompt` change of `workspace --event prompt` (`busy = true`, `tools_running = 0`, view marker and the line of a persistent condition as there). It waits for `activity.lock` at most the smaller of 1 s and the time left of its 4.5 s verb wait, so the hook still ends within 5 s (§17.1); a lock not taken, or no time left, is added to its one stderr line (C6) and the turn is not marked. `workspace --event prompt` (members only): a line that `prompt` consumes as a verb (a `/zygo:` line; the `zygo` binary always registers the dispatch; the one rule of both hooks, `prompt::consumes`) changes nothing (a verb that fails blocks the line with an error text; the line of a verb that panics is marked by `prompt`, above); any other line, in the main loop: `busy = true`, `tools_running = 0`; during `stopping` while `down` holds `op.lock`: the block of AU86. |
| `PreToolUse` | `*` | `workspace --event pre-tool` | The deny of W§9.2 for the subagent tool (`Agent`). Else, main loop: `tools_running += 1`. A denied call does not count. |
| `PostToolUse` | `*` | `workspace --event tool-end` | Main loop: `tools_running -= 1`, never below 0. |
| `PostToolUseFailure` | `*` | `workspace --event tool-end` | As `PostToolUse`. |
| `Stop` | — | `workspace --event stop` | Main loop: `busy = false` (AU85), `tools_running = 0`. |
| `SessionEnd` | — | `workspace --event session-end` | Main loop: `tools_running = 0`. |
| `StopFailure` | — | `workspace --event stop` | As `Stop` (AU57). |
| `SubagentStart` | — | `workspace --event subagent-start` | Adds `agent_id`, `agent_type` to the running set. |
| `SubagentStop` | — | `workspace --event subagent-stop` | Removes `agent_id`. |

`activity.json` schema (version 1), changed only under `activity.lock` (AU56):

| Field | Type | Written by |
|---|---|---|
| `version` | `1` | every write |
| `busy` | bool | `UserPromptSubmit` (true); `Stop`, `StopFailure` (false); main loop only (AU43). OpenCode: the plugin's `prompt` (true) and `stop` (false) calls (AU27); the `session` call does not change it |
| `running` | map `agent_id` → `agent_type` | `SubagentStart` (add), `SubagentStop` (remove) |
| `tools_running` | u32, absent when 0 | `PreToolUse` (+1), `PostToolUse` and `PostToolUseFailure` (-1, never below 0); `UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `StopFailure` and `SessionEnd` (0); main loop only. OpenCode (AU27 tool count): the plugin's `pre-tool` calls (+1: each tool other than `task` of a session that is not a known child, and the `Agent` call of `task` in any session when it is not denied), its `tool-end` calls (-1: at most one from `tool.execute.after` for each counted call), and its `prompt` and `stop` calls (0; the plugin then forgets every counted call); the `session` call does not change it. Above 0 the member is busy whatever `busy` holds (AU26). A file without it reads 0; in the file of a newer zygo a value that is not a number reads 1 |
| `updated_at` | Unix time | every write |

It holds no tool input and no text. A missing file means `busy = false`, an empty running set and `tools_running = 0`. A member whose agent sends no member events (`launch.events_off`, or hooks off by a merged `disableAllHooks: true`) never writes the file; the drain counts it as busy by AU26, not by this file. The hooks find the workspace through the session view's membership marker (AU58). `view.json` has two readers, and on unix neither waits on a FIFO (FIX-22). `SessionView::load` (`session-start`, `trim`, the status line, the MCP tools, the work guards and hooks) opens it with `O_NONBLOCK` and follows a link; a FIFO is the error `<view.json path>: is a FIFO`, for every launch, member or not; every other entry gives the result of a plain read, and for a device that waits at open, such as a tty with no carrier, the wait moves from the open to the read. `read_view` (`read_session_file`, `crates/zygo-hooks/src/workspace.rs`: the member event hooks of `zygo hook workspace` and `verb_member`) opens it with `O_NOFOLLOW` and `O_NONBLOCK`; a missing file is no view, a link is the error `<view.json path>: is a link; not read`, and a FIFO or any other entry that is not a regular file is `<view.json path>: is not a regular file; not read`. Every failure follows C6.

Notes:

- The pinned Claude Code (2.1.328) names the subagent tool `Agent` (`fixtures/claude-code/hooks/PreToolUse-Agent-main.json`).
- Before L2, zygo registered `SubagentStop` only with work units on (`ClaudeCode::hooks`), and no `Stop`, `StopFailure`, `SubagentStart`, `Notification` or `SessionEnd` hook. A member gets the new entries whether work units are on or off. Two `SubagentStop` entries (work and workspace) both run.
- The `*` matchers of `PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse` and `PostToolUseFailure` add two short `zygo` processes per tool call of a member, one at its start and one at its end, and none for a launch that is not a member.
- A member's `UserPromptSubmit` runs two short `zygo` processes, `prompt` and `workspace --event prompt`; a launch that is not a member runs one.

OpenCode: AU104, AU27. The generated plugin (§8.7) runs these calls only when `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` is set: a cheap gate in the agent process, set by the launcher for members only; the hooks still decide from the view marker only (AU58). At load the plugin sends `zygo hook workspace --agent opencode --event session` with `{"cwd": <directory>}`, which commits the pending start. Each event is classified and counted by AU27: the kind of a session comes only from the `info` of a `session.*` event (`parentID` present and not null: a child), and a `session.created` event or a `session.status` event with status `busy` activates a session. The main session is the known top session activated last that is not deleted; each change of it sends `--event session` with `{"sessionID": <id>, "cwd": <directory>}`. A `session.deleted` event marks its session deleted and removes it from the turn set; a deleted session is never sent after its `session.deleted` event. A record made before the delete is kept when no other known top session exists (`up` then tries it, AU12); otherwise the record moves to the newest known top session that is not deleted (known limit: a session deleted with no `session.deleted` event stays recorded; no fixture shows the event). The turn set holds the sessions in a turn that are not known children: when it becomes non-empty the plugin sends `--event prompt` (the turn start, as `UserPromptSubmit`), when it becomes empty `--event stop` (the turn end, as `Stop`), each with the session id that changed it and the same payload form. A `retry` status, a repeated signal and the events of a known child send nothing. The plugin decides each call when the event arrives, before any wait, and runs the calls one after another, so they reach zygo in that order; the `event` handler does not wait for them. `tool.execute.before` on the tool `task`, in every session, queues `--event pre-tool` with `{"sessionID": <id>, "tool_name": "Agent", "cwd": <directory>}`, waits for it (at most 10 s for each call queued ahead plus 10 s for its own; each tool call of a member queues up to two calls, so the wait grows with the tool calls queued ahead), and a deny throws its reason before the guard runs. Every other tool of a session that is not a known child queues `--event pre-tool` with `{"sessionID": <id>, "tool_name": <tool>, "cwd": <directory>}` before the guard and does not wait. `tool.execute.after` of a counted call (AU27 tool count) queues `--event tool-end` with `{"sessionID": <id>, "cwd": <directory>}` before `trim` and `work`. These calls go through the same queue as `session`, `prompt` and `stop`. Event names: PW4 (OpenCode 1.19.6, `fixtures/opencode/workspace/`); the `busy` status type: the `SessionStatus` schema of OpenCode 1.19.6 (`idle`, `retry`, `busy`), not in a fixture (E2 re-checks it). Known limits: AU27; `opencode run --attach <url>` and `opencode attach <url>` run the plugin in a server without the member environment, and `--pure` loads no plugin; these send no member events, and such a member counts as busy for the drain until its agent exits, the drain timeout or `down --now` (AU26, AU27 known limit 8).

### 9.2 Dispatch and stopping blocks

While a `down` holds `op.lock` (AU83):

- `draining`, with `drain_block_dispatch = true`: the `pre-tool` event on tool `Agent` prints `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"PreToolUse","permissionDecision":"deny","permissionDecisionReason":"zygo: workspace is draining for down; finish your turn"}}`.
- `stopping`: the `pre-tool` event on `Agent` denies with `zygo: workspace is stopping`, and `UserPromptSubmit` prints `{"decision":"block","reason":"zygo: workspace is stopping"}` (AU86).

The hook reads `phase` from `workspace.json` without `lock` (the file is replaced by rename). In any other case it prints nothing and does not touch `op.lock`.

### 9.3 Sequence

1. Steps 1-3 of W§8.1 (platform, tmux, workspace). With no name, the workspace of the cwd checkout; no environment value overrides it. `/zygo:down` and the `workspace_down` tool name the workspace of the session's membership marker, never the cwd; a session with no marker gets `zygo: this session is not in a workspace` (exit 1). With `--all`: every workspace (AU19, AU80).
2. Detach rule (AU18). `$TMUX_PANE` is looked up in the session of every target: a target with a saved socket only when that socket is the socket of `$TMUX`, a target with no saved socket on the server of `$TMUX` (else tmux's default). Before any tmux call on a saved socket in this step, the AU54 check of that socket runs (exit 2 with the AU54 line), then the AU62 check; a socket that is not private exits 1 with the AU62 line. When it applies: the sandbox guard of AU18 first, before any session check (it reads the server pid; an unreadable pid refuses); then, when no target session exists, `down` runs in the foreground (step 3 needs no drain); else make the zygo path absolute against the cwd (the detached process runs in `/`) and check that joined path (AU54; exit 2), cut the log (AU97) and start `zygo down` again as a new session leader (`setsid`) with the same `--now` and `--timeout`, plus `--wait`, and the same target (`--all`, or `--` and the name); stdin from `/dev/null`, stdout and stderr to the log of AU97. The started process keeps no descriptor of the caller's stdin, stdout or stderr (the `workspace_down` tool waits for their end, AU65). The child closes every descriptor above 2 at its `exec`: before it, each one is marked close-on-exec, so no descriptor that the caller left inheritable reaches the started process and a failed `exec` is still reported. Print `zygo: down started; end your turn` and exit 0.
3. Per workspace: take `op.lock` (AU83 retry); held: report `up or down is in progress` (with `--all`, skip; exit 1 at the end). Repair as W§8.1 step 5. Check the session without `lock`: gone by AU87 → under `lock`, `phase = down` (and `down_all = true` with `--all`; no other `down` changes `down_all`; a state already in phase `down` gets no write), report `zygo: <name> is not running`, release `op.lock`; unreachable, another `@zygo-ws` or another tmux error → exit 1. Else, under `lock`, `phase = draining`.
4. Final layout snapshot with `--force` (W§7.4, AU38). A snapshot that refuses what it read (one `errors.log` line: a `@zygo-pane` that fails AU84, a key on two panes, an unexpected tmux error) stops `down` before anything stops: `phase = up`, release `op.lock`, exit 1 (W§18 #10). An `Err` because `snapshot.pending` could not be set prints one line and `down` goes on.
5. Drain (skip with `--now`). Poll every 500 ms. For each member whose launcher is live, read `activity.json` and the proxy status (session token from its session directory; AU91), and apply the idle rule (AU26). A member whose agent sends no member events (AU26: `launch.events_off`, or hooks off) is busy at every poll. The state is read again at each poll, so a member that joins during the drain is drained too. The status of each live member is read with a 2 s bound. Each workspace's drain time starts after its own step 4. With `--all`, a workspace that waits in step 10 does not stop the polls and timeouts of the others. A member whose launcher is not live counts as idle. Foreground progress: one line per member when it becomes idle, and a line every 30 s with the members that still run. With `--all`, every workspace drains under one timeout, and each one goes on to step 7 as soon as all its members are idle in the same poll (AU19).
6. Timeout (`--timeout`, else `drain_timeout`): for each workspace still draining, set `phase = up`, which ends the dispatch block, and release its `op.lock`. Print each busy member with its pane, its running subagents and its reason: the busy reason of the idle rule (AU26), `member events off: launched with --bare, --pure or attach` or `hooks off: its --settings sets disableAllHooks` for a member whose agent sends no member events (AU26), or `no Stop since its last prompt` for a member whose turn is still open. Then print `run zygo down --now to stop at once`. Exit 1.
7. Set `phase = stopping` (AU86). From here SIGINT, SIGHUP and SIGTERM do not end `down` for this workspace, and it never goes back. One handler, installed once per `down` before its first `draining` write and restored when `down` ends, catches the three signals.
8. For each member whose launcher is live (AU74): record `closed_units` (AU31, AU76); with `--now`, record the running set as `interrupted` (ids and types); set `clean_stop = true` and `saved_for` = the ULID of that member's live zygo session (the session folder that AU74 found live). Write the state. These values are saved only here (AU42).
9. Warn about busy shell panes (AU24).
10. For each member whose launcher is live: send SIGTERM to its launcher (pid from its session directory `pid` file). The launcher forwards it to the agent (`signals.rs:105-130`) and runs `finish` (`launcher.rs:729-754`). Wait until each such member record has left `running` and its launcher is no longer live, at most 30 s; then report the ones still running and continue. No SIGKILL follows: `kill-session` in step 11 sends SIGHUP to every pane process, and a SIGKILL would cut the launcher's `finish`.
11. Check `@zygo-ws` (AU63) and run `kill-session -t =zygo-<name>`. Then, under `lock`: `phase = down`, and `down_all = true` with `--all`. Release `op.lock`. Print `zygo: <name> is down (<n> agents saved)`. A `down` that dies between the kill and the write leaves `stopping` with no session; the repair of AU75 sets `phase = down`.

`op.lock` is released on every exit path (an fs4 lock also ends with its process). SIGINT, SIGHUP or SIGTERM from before the `draining` write until the `stopping` write cancels every drain that is not stopping yet: `phase = up`, release `op.lock`, exit 128 + the signal number (130 for Ctrl+C). With `--all`, workspaces that already stopped or are stopping go on to the end. With `--all`, a signal before a workspace's step 3 leaves it and the workspaces after it untouched, and `down` exits 128 + the signal number.

### 9.4 Resume note

When `note_pending = true`, `ws run` passes the note inputs in `LaunchOpts.resume_note`, and the launcher puts them in the session view that it builds (AU72). At the first main-loop `SessionStart`, the session-start hook builds the note from them, adds it after the session text (§8.6), removes them from the session view, and clears the saved values in the member record (AU60):

- With `clean_stop = false` (crash, reboot or hand kill; AU42): `zygo did not stop this session cleanly; the list of closed units was not saved.`
- With interrupted subagents: `zygo stopped this session while subagents <type> <id>, <type> <id> were running; their results were lost; dispatch them again if still needed.`
- With closed units: `Units <unit>, <unit> were closed when zygo stopped this session; call work_begin to open them again if work continues.`

No note when `clean_stop` is `true` and both lists are empty.

The note follows the session text after one empty line, one sentence per line in the order above; with `clean_stop = false` only the first sentence is printed. An empty `<type>` prints `<id>` alone. Each type, id and unit is printed with control characters escaped. Claude Code: the hook prints the note first, then removes the inputs from the session view (also when no note is printed) and clears the member record (AU60). OpenCode: the plugin adds the session text to each model request, so the note is in the system text of every request while its inputs are in the session view. The inputs belong to one agent session: their `agent_session` is the resumed id, or none for a fresh start. A `session` call with id X (AU104): inputs with no `agent_session` get X and stay; inputs with X stay; inputs with another id are removed from the session view. The commit (AU92) never removes them.

## 10. Failure handling

| Case | Behavior |
|---|---|
| A write fails partway | The rename did not happen, so the old `workspace.json` stays whole (W§7.3). A hook writes one line to `errors.log` and exits 0 (C6). |
| `workspace.json` does not parse or fails AU84 | `up` and `down` exit 1 naming the file; `--all` skips it (AU80). `up --reset` renames it to `workspace.json.bad` and applies the loadout. Hooks and snapshots change nothing and write one line to `errors.log` (AU84, W§7.4 step 1). |
| `workspace.json` larger than 1 MiB | Refused and never changed (AU84, AU29): `up`, `up --reset`, `ws forget <id>` and `ws forget-pane` exit 1 with `zygo: <path>: state file too large; it may be from a newer zygo, so zygo does not change it`; `--all` skips it (AU80); `zygo workspace` gives that text; `ls` shows it by id as `unreadable`. Hooks and snapshots change nothing. |
| `up <name>` or `attach <name>` with no state of that name (a state that cannot be read counts when the `name` of its JSON matches) | Exit 1, `zygo: no workspace named <name>` (AU20). |
| Lock held | Bounded waits (W§7.3). An fs4 lock ends with its holder, so a lock is never stale. |
| `up` or `down` process died | `op.lock` is free while `phase` is `building`, `draining` or `stopping`. `up` and `down` repair (W§8.1 step 5, AU67, AU75). The blocks of W§9.2 end at once (AU83). |
| tmux server gone (crash, reboot, `kill-server`) | The launchers received SIGHUP or SIGTERM, so members stay `resumable` (AU17). `phase` stays `up`. `ls` shows `stopped (tmux gone)`. `up` restores the last written layout (W§7.5 rule 2). Closed units and interrupted subagents are not saved; the resume note says so (AU42). |
| tmux unreachable while launchers live (another `TMUX_TMPDIR`, a sandbox) | Not "gone": the verb exits 1 and resumes nothing (AU87, W-C9). |
| Workspace session killed by hand | As "tmux server gone" (AU89). |
| One pane closed while the session lives | Detached member (W§7.5 rule 1). |
| A split fails at `up` (no space) | The pane is not built; its member is detached and stays resumable (AU96). |
| tmux answers with an unexpected error | No change; exit 1 or one `errors.log` line (AU87). |
| No agent session id saved | The pane comes back as a shell; `up` deletes its member record when the record has no agent session id and no pending start (AU53). `up` prints `zygo: pane <key>: no agent session id saved; started a shell`. |
| Resume fails | A shell and the resume command; the next `up` tries again (AU12, W§8.2 step 7). |
| Missing cwd | AU61, W§8.2 step 1. |
| Repository moved or deleted | The common-dir path changes, so the id changes and `up` creates a new workspace. `ls` shows the old one as `repo missing`. `zygo ws forget <name>` deletes it. |
| Profile, server or binary missing at resume | The launcher's existing messages apply (`launcher.rs:292` for a profile; exit 13 for a missing agent); the member stays as it was (AU60, AU92). |
| Newer state version | AU29. |
| Two `up` at the same time | `op.lock`: the second exits 1 after the 200 ms retry. |

## 11. Configuration

User config only (AU10, AU22). Schema additions in `crates/zygo-config/src/schema.rs` (`Config` at `:13-36`): `workspace: WorkspaceConfig`, `loadout: BTreeMap<String, Loadout>`.

```toml
[workspace]
default_loadout = "solo"        # built-in
drain_block_dispatch = true     # OP6
drain_timeout = "10m"           # AU4; <n>s | <n>m | <n>h
pool = "~/.claude-interns"      # optional; config-directory pool (workspace definition design R§9.5); absolute after ~ expansion

[loadout.pair]
[[loadout.pair.window]]
name = "agents"
layout = "main-vertical"        # even-horizontal | even-vertical | main-horizontal | main-vertical | tiled | a tmux layout string
[[loadout.pair.window.pane]]
kind = "claude-code"            # claude-code | opencode | shell
job = "worker"                  # orchestrator | worker; optional; absent: no job (R§9.3)
profile = "cheap"               # model profile (§11); optional
args = ["--model", "lead"]      # agent arguments after `--`; optional
yolo = false                    # optional
passthrough = false             # claude-code only; optional
server = "https://zygo.example" # optional; as `--server`; no user info (AU98)
compact = "600k"                # optional; as `--compact` (text, parsed by parse_window)
config_dir = "~/.claude-work"   # claude-code only; optional; as `--config-dir`
pool = false                    # claude-code only; optional; not with config_dir (R§9.5)
[[loadout.pair.window.pane]]
kind = "shell"
cwd = "branch:feature-x"        # optional; default the main worktree
```

- `drain_timeout` that is not `<n>s`, `<n>m` or `<n>h`: `down` prints one warning line and uses 10 min.
- `cwd`: an absolute path, a path relative to the main worktree, or `branch:<name>`. `branch:` resolves to the worktree that has that branch checked out (`git worktree list --porcelain`). No such worktree: the main worktree and one warning line. zygo does not create worktrees.
- Built-in `solo`: one window, one `claude-code` pane, no arguments, main worktree.
- Agent pane fields map to `LaunchOpts` as AU45 says. A field that is not set takes the default of `zygo claude` or `zygo opencode`.
- `job` and `pool` (workspace definition design R§9.3, R§9.5). A pane with no `job` has no job; zygo does not guess one. A pane with `pool = true` takes its config directory from `[workspace] pool` at a fresh build and runs in passthrough; with no `[workspace] pool` it starts as any other pane. The pane key `name` is defined in the workspace mail design (M§6).
- `zygo config check` validates loadouts in two places. `crates/zygo-config/src/check.rs`: a known `kind`; `passthrough`, `config_dir` and `pool` only with `claude-code`; `pool` and `config_dir` not both set; `job` is `orchestrator` or `worker`; at most one pane with `job = "orchestrator"` per loadout; at least one pane per window; `default_loadout` names a loadout; `[workspace] pool` absolute after the `~` expansion; a missing `profile` is a warning (the launch continues without it, as today). `crates/zygo/src/cmd/config.rs`, for `claude-code` and `opencode` panes (the checks that need the compact parser, AU30 or AU98, which `zygo-config` does not depend on, AU103): a `compact` that the launch refuses is an error, `on` included, as `zygo claude` and `zygo opencode` refuse it (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/claude.rs:32-34`, `crates/zygo/src/cmd/opencode.rs:32-34`); a `server` with user info is an error (AU98); `args` that AU30 would remove, taken relative to the pane's folder, give one warning that names each removed argument and never its value (W§12); a `--settings` is not reported (AU59). The `compact` error line shows the value as `zygo config show` shows that field (W§12: the rule for one argument); no other check line prints a config value.
- `[loadout]` and `[workspace]` in `.zygo.toml` are ignored by the existing filter, with its existing warning (`repo_file.rs:218-228`, `crates/zygo-config/src/layers.rs:461-469`). In the team layer zygo drops them with `zygo: [loadout] and [workspace] are read from user config only; ignored`.
- The builder confirms how an older zygo treats the new tables in a shared user config.

## 12. CLI

Checked against the existing verbs (`crates/zygo/src/cmd/mod.rs:131-241`): no clash.

| Command | Does |
|---|---|
| `zygo up [--loadout <n>] [--reset] [--no-attach] [--all] [--] [<name>]` | W§8. |
| `zygo down [--now] [--timeout <dur>] [--wait] [--all] [--] [<name>]` | W§9. Also `/zygo:down [--now]`: the workspace of this session's membership marker (never the cwd; none: exit 1), detached (OP11, AU18). The detached process gets the name after `--`. Also the MCP tool `workspace_down` (`now`) when `[features] workspace_tool` is on (AU65). |
| `zygo workspace` | Status of the workspace of this session (`ZYGO_WORKSPACE`), else of the cwd's checkout: `workspace <name>: <phase>`, then one line per member in pane key order, `<pane key> <kind> <state> <agent session id or ->`, or `no members`. Exit 1 with one of: `invalid workspace id <id>` (a `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` that fails AU84), `no workspace with id <id>` (a valid `ZYGO_WORKSPACE` with no state), `no workspace for this session or folder` (no `ZYGO_WORKSPACE`, and the cwd is in no checkout or in one with no state); a state that cannot be read gives its error. A symbolic link in place of the state directory counts as no state; a state file larger than 1 MiB gives `<path>: state file too large; it may be from a newer zygo, so zygo does not change it` (AU84). Also `/zygo:workspace` (OP11). |
| `zygo ls` | One line per workspace, in name order: `<name>  <main worktree>  <state>  agents <running>/<saved>/<detached>  clients <attached clients>  unread <unread messages of all member records>`, `-` for a value that is not known; with no workspace, the one line `no workspaces`. State: `up`, `down`, `building`, `draining`, `stopping` (the saved phase); `stopped (tmux gone)` (phase `up`, session gone, no live launcher); `unreachable` (phase `up` and the session cannot be confirmed: a live launcher with the session gone (AU87), a session without this workspace's `@zygo-ws` (AU63), a socket that fails AU54 or AU62 (no tmux call), or no saved socket); `repo missing` (phase `down` or session gone, and `main_root` or `common_dir` is not a folder); `unreadable` (the state does not parse, fails AU84 or is newer; the name column shows the workspace id); `unknown (<reason>)` (phase `up` and no answer: `no tmux`, `tmux timed out`, `tmux error`). Running: members whose launcher is live; saved: members in state `running` or `resumable` with an agent session id, no live launcher and a pane in `panes[]`; detached: the same with no pane (W§7.5). Attached clients only for a session of this workspace. `ls` makes no tmux call after 4 s from its start; a socket whose call failed or timed out is not asked again in the same `ls`. Also `/zygo:ls` (OP11). |
| `zygo attach [--] [<name>]` | Attach or switch to a running workspace: the AU54 check of `tmux_socket` (exit 2), the AU62 check of `tmux_socket`, then the AU87 session check, then AU63; AU100 for another server. Not running: exit 1, `zygo: <name> is down; run zygo up -- <name>`. |
| `zygo loadout list` / `zygo loadout show <n>` | Loadouts with their source (built-in or user); one loadout in TOML. `zygo loadout show` and `zygo config show` run one function on each loadout argument and each loadout text field, and each output line goes through the same control-character escape (`zygo_core::esc`: C0 and C1 controls and bidi controls as `\u{..}`); `loadout show` has no other step, so both print the same bytes for each argument and field. Words: the secret words are `key`, `token`, `secret`, `password`, `auth`, `bearer` and `cookie`, compared without case (`session` is not one: `--session-id` and `--resume` values are no secrets); a name is one or more ASCII letters, digits, `_`, `-` and `.`; a secret name is a name that holds a secret word; a blank is a space or a tab. The rule for one text (an argument, or a field): (1) user info: the text is `<user info removed>` when it holds a scheme of 2 or more characters followed by `:/` or `:\`, or a one-letter scheme followed by `://`, with an `@` anywhere after it (`?`, `#` and `/` do not end the search); when the text, its `=` value or its attached short-flag value has the form `user:pass@host` (the text before its last `@` holds a `:` and no `/` or `\`); or when the text starts with `user:pass@host` (no `/` or blank before its first `:`, then an `@` with no blank between that `:` and the `@`; a `/` there does not matter), unless it starts with a drive letter (`C:\`, or `C:/` not followed by a second `/`; `x://` is a scheme). (2) Inline JSON: a text, `=` value or attached short-flag value whose first non-blank character is `{` or `[` is `<inline JSON not shown>`, also when it does not parse (`-d [ … ]` gives `-d<inline JSON not shown>`). (3) Secret names: in the text before its first `?` or `#`, the first secret name that is followed by `=` or `:` (blanks may come before and after it), or by blanks and more text, ends the text: what follows is `<value not shown>` (`API_KEY = <value not shown>`, `Authorization: <value not shown>`, `GITHUB_TOKEN <value not shown>`, `--env=PATH=/usr/bin:AUTH_TOKEN=<value not shown>`, `--api-key=<value not shown>`). (4) Parameters: from the first `?` or `#` of the text (with or without a scheme), the text is a list of parameters split on `&`, `;`, `?` and `#`; a parameter `name=value` whose name, percent-decoded, holds a secret word is `name=<value not shown>` with the name as typed (`?to%6Ben=<value not shown>`, `#access_token=<value not shown>`); every other value goes through this rule again. Arguments: each value of an AU30 content flag or of `--settings` before the `--` that ends the flags (by the arities of the pinned help; every `--mcp-config` value) is shown only when it names a regular file, taken relative to the pane's folder as `zygo up` takes it (AU30), else `<inline JSON not shown>`; a shown content value goes through rules (1), (3) and (4). Every other argument, also after that `--`, goes through the rule. A secret flag is an argument that starts with `-` and whose text before any `=` is a secret name; when it has no `=` or an empty `=` value, the next argument is its value and is `<value not shown>`, also when it starts with `-`, except: `--`; a secret flag, which is shown as a flag and takes its own next argument (`--auth --api-key <value not shown>`); and an argument that starts with `--` and is a flag of the agent's table (`crates/zygo-workspace/src/args.rs`), which is shown and read as that flag (`--api-key --mcp-config m.json`). Fields: a loadout `server` that holds user info (AU98) is `<user info removed>`; every other text field (`kind`, `profile`, `server`, `compact`, `config_dir`, `cwd`, pane `name` (M§6) and `job` (R§9.1), window `name` and `layout`) goes through the rule as one argument; a `cwd` of the form `branch:<name>` is `branch:` and `<name>` through the rule, so `branch:alice@fix-1` is shown. The `zygo config check` `compact` line shows the value by the same rule (W§11). So `me@example.com`, `claude-sonnet-4@20250514`, `./a@b`, `/x/y@z`, `git@github.com:org/repo`, `C:\Users\first.last@corp\x.json`, `--session-id <id>` and `keyboard.json` are shown. Known limits: the value after a bare short flag (`-t x`) is shown, because the meaning of an unknown short flag cannot be known; after a secret flag followed by an unknown flag (`--secret --x S`), `--x` is taken as the value and hidden, and `S` is a positional and is shown; some values that are no secret are hidden (accepted): `node:20@sha256:…` and `openai:gpt@v1` by the `user:pass@host` rule, `ssh://git@host/…` by the scheme rule, names such as `--author`, `--max-tokens`, `--keybindings` and `monkey=…` and a host with a secret word before a port (`https://keyvault.example:443`) by the secret-name rules; the pinned help of Claude Code and OpenCode has no flag or example whose value these rules hide (test `help_fixture_words_that_the_rules_change`). `zygo config check` takes each pane's arguments relative to the pane's folder, as `zygo up` does, and names each argument that AU30 removes by its flag (`-c` and `-r` for `-cr`; a flag that loses only a value is named too), `--`, `<after -->` or `<positional>`, never by a value. The pane folder is looked up only when the output can show a loadout: `zygo loadout show`, `zygo config check`, and `zygo config show` with no `--layer` or with `--layer user` (only the user layer holds `[loadout]`). When it cannot be found, they print `zygo: the pane folder could not be found (<error>); paths are checked against the current folder` and go on. |
| `zygo ws forget [--] <name>` | `<name>` is matched first as a workspace id (16 hex characters, with that state directory present), then as a workspace name, then against the JSON `name` of a state from a newer zygo. Only a real directory is a state directory: a symbolic link at `<state dir>/workspaces/<id>` is never opened, locked, written or changed; `forget <id>` removes the link itself (an unlink, after one more check that it is a link) and prints `zygo: <path> was a link, not a state folder; removed the link`. For a state directory, `forget` takes `op.lock`, reads the state again, runs its checks, and takes `lock` (5 s) before it deletes anything; then, under both locks, it renames the state directory out of the workspace list first, then deletes the workspace cache (results and program folders), then removes the renamed directory. A failure before the rename deletes nothing, and the same `forget` runs again. A cache that cannot be deleted after the rename gives `zygo: <name> forgotten, but <cache> was not removed: <error>` (exit 1); the next `forget` deletes it as a cache with no state directory. The state goes before the cache so that a `ws run` that made a program folder after the read of W§8.2 step 1 always finds one of two things when it reads the state again (AU61): the state still there, and then the cache deletion comes after its folder and deletes it; or the state gone, and then it deletes its folder itself. No order of the two leaves a folder. Refused (exit 1, nothing deleted) while `op.lock` is held, while `lock` stays held past 5 s, while a member launcher is live, while the workspace's own session runs (`@zygo-ws` equal to its id; a session of another workspace under that name does not refuse it, AU63), when the socket fails AU62 (checked also when tmux is not installed), when the session check gives a tmux error, for a state from a newer zygo (AU29), and for a name that two states share (the message lists their ids). Right before its first tmux call (the session check), `forget` checks the socket of that call by AU54: the saved `tmux_socket` (also the raw one of a state that cannot be read), else the socket of `$TMUX`. A failure refuses with exit 2 and nothing deleted. For a saved socket the line is `zygo: the zygo path or tmux socket path holds a quote, a backslash, a $ or a control character; workspaces cannot use it; remove <state folder> by hand to forget this workspace` (the path escaped, C6), because `up` and `down` refuse that state too; for the socket of `$TMUX` it is the AU54 line alone. A socket whose folder is missing, or no tmux, gives no tmux call, so no AU54 check: `forget` goes on. A state that does not parse or fails AU84 is checked from its raw JSON, read with the AU84 cap (name, socket, member launchers, session); when a check finds it running, or no check can run, `forget` refuses and names `zygo up --reset` as the repair. A state file larger than 1 MiB is refused by id with the AU84 text before any lock, and is not matched by name. A symbolic link at the cache path or in the state directory is removed as a link. Before its own work, `forget` removes folders that an earlier `forget` left (`.forget-*`), state directories with no `workspace.json` whose `op.lock` and `lock` are free, and results caches whose id has no state directory (none, or a link or file in its place) or a state directory with no `workspace.json` whose locks are free. `ls` removes nothing. |
| `zygo ws forget-pane <key>` | Delete one member record (detached or not) and, in the same write, set that pane's `panes[].kind` to `shell` (AU53); then the `snapshot.pending` check of W§7.3; then delete its results cache `results/<key>/` and its program folder `program/<key>/` (AU61). The record goes before the caches, for the reason given at `zygo ws forget`: a `ws run` that reads the state again after it made its program folder finds the record gone, or the cache deletion still to come. Each part of each cache path below the workspace cache directory (`<id>`, `results` or `program`, `<key>`) is checked without following links, twice. The first check, before the record write, refuses before anything is written: a link at `<id>`, `results` or `program`, or a part that cannot be read, gives exit 1 with nothing changed. The second check runs at the deletion, for each cache in turn; a link at `<key>` is removed as a link. Every cache is tried, also after an error; then the first error is the line (`zygo: cannot forget pane <key> of <name>: <error>`, exit 1). The record is then gone and what is left is a cache only, so a second run of the same command deletes the rest (the rule below for a key with no member record). Refused (exit 1, nothing changed) while its launcher is live (checked under `lock`), while `op.lock` is held, while `lock` stays held past 5 s, for a key that fails AU84, and when the workspace's state directory is a symbolic link (`zygo: the state folder of workspace <id> is a link; it is not changed`). A saved `tmux_socket` that fails AU54 does not refuse `forget-pane`: the record goes, and the `snapshot.pending` check writes its one `errors.log` line with no tmux call (W§7.4 step 1). A key with no member record but a results cache or a program folder only removes those. Run in the workspace's checkout or with `--name <name>` (a workspace id is matched first, as for `ws forget`). A state file larger than 1 MiB is refused with the AU84 text, by `--name <id>` or in its checkout, and is not matched by name. |
| `zygo ws snapshot --ws-id <id> [--event <hook name>] [--force]`, `zygo ws run --ws-id <id> --pane <key> [--shell]`, `zygo hook workspace` | Internal and hidden (AU44). No sweep for `ws` (AU71). |

Exit codes follow §8.2: `0`, `1` failure or busy, `2` usage or platform. `zygo doctor` also reports the tmux version and whether it meets the minimum. Slash forms: `/zygo:down`, `/zygo:ls`, `/zygo:workspace` (OP11); `up`, `attach`, `loadout` and `ws` are CLI-only. The slash forms run under the 4.5 s hook bound of §17.1; `/zygo:down` returns after it starts the detached process.

## 13. Multiplexer trait

`crates/zygo-workspace` defines one trait. tmux is the only phase-2 implementation (OP5). The trait has the operations of W§7.4, W§8 and W§9: check version, socket path, session exists (with the AU87 answer kinds), create session with a first pane, a start directory, a size, a cleaned environment and, in the same tmux invocation, the session options `@zygo-ws` and `destroy-unattached off` and the first pane's `@zygo-pane`, set and hide (`-r`) session environment values, set and read session options, set and unset window options, set hooks, create window with a first pane, rename window, split pane with a start command (argument list), set and read pane options, apply layout, select window and pane, zoom, list panes with fields (AU99), attach, switch client, kill session. Every call except `attach` has a 2 s bound. After creation, the tmux implementation passes `-S <tmux_socket>` to every call (AU62) and escapes `#` in every `-c` and `-n` value (AU55). Every call except `new_session` and `attach` runs under `LC_ALL=C` (AU87) with `-u`, because a client under `LC_ALL=C` without `-u` prints each non-ASCII character of a window name or a pane path as `_` (W3 review probe, tmux 3.7c). `new_session` runs with exactly the cleaned environment of AU64 and no `LC_ALL=C`, because a server that it starts copies the client's environment into the global environment of every pane, and tmux marks each `update-environment` name that the client lacks as removed in the new session. `attach` has no bound, because an attached client lasts as long as the user works, and keeps the caller's environment, because tmux reads the locale to draw on the user's terminal. Option reads use `show-options -q`, so an unset option, and also a missing target, gives an empty value. Tests use it with a private server.

## 14. Alternatives rejected

| Alternative | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| A supervisor daemon | Reduced scope **(operator)**. tmux already holds the processes, and hooks plus launchers can write the state (AU1). A daemon adds install and lifecycle work (§18.3). It may return in a later phase. |
| Messages, wake, triggers, git modes, pools, slots, toasts, restart briefs | Reduced scope **(operator)**. Elephant-goldfish subagents make cross-agent coordination unnecessary for now. They may return in a later phase. |
| Fresh restart by default | **(operator, OP4)**. The operator wants each agent to continue its conversation. A fresh restart loses the lead's plan and context. |
| A Windows Terminal backend | Windows Terminal has no scriptable layout query or session hooks to snapshot from. OP5 keeps the trait, so a later backend is possible. |
| A one-agent Windows mode | It gives a second, smaller feature set to keep in step. WSL gives the full feature. |
| Auto-tmux on `zygo claude` | Breaks OP9: a plain launch must stay a v1 launch. Only `zygo up` creates a workspace. |
| Loadouts in `.zygo.toml` | Still rejected for `.zygo.toml`: it is applied with no approval (§13.5), and a loadout starts programs with arguments (W-C5, A5). A repository loadout comes from `.zygo/workspace.toml`, behind the approval of workspace definition design R§7 (operator). |
| Periodic-only snapshots | They lose changes made between ticks, and they need a timer process. Event hooks plus a final snapshot at `down` cover OP7 without a daemon. |
| tmux-resurrect or tmux-continuum | They run on the whole server (against W-C3), they re-run saved commands (against OP8), and they do not know agent session ids. |
| Claude Code background sessions (`--bg`, `respawn`, `fixtures/claude-code/help.txt`) | Claude Code only; the session runs outside the tmux layout, and the zygo launcher (with its proxy) is not its parent. |
| A shell string as pane command (`$SHELL -c '…; exec $SHELL'`) | Quoting differs between sh, zsh and fish. An argument list plus `exec` (AU15) needs no quoting. |
| Key members by tmux pane id (`%N`) | Pane ids change after a restore. A pane key in a user option stays the same. |
| `down` waits in the foreground when an agent runs it | It waits for its own caller's turn and kills its own pane, and an agent's Bash tool times out (AU18). |
| Refuse `down` while a shell pane runs a program | `down` can run with no person at the terminal; a refusal blocks W§1 reason 1 (AU24). |
| `up` holds the lock while it creates panes | Agent panes start during that time and must write their member records; they would wait for `up` (AU36). |
| Snapshot deletes the record of each pane that is gone | A `kill-session` closes panes one by one while the session still exists, so every member would be lost (W§7.5). |
| A stable layout with candidates and promotion; tombstones for `kill-pane` | They needed hook-order rules and extra fields. The 2 s shrink check (AU89) plus detached members give the protection with no stored history. |
| Save launch arguments unfiltered | They can hold prompt text and session arguments that clash with the added `--resume` (AU30, W-C7). |
| Strip provider keys from tmux and `ws run` | The in-process proxy of each pane's launcher reads them (`crates/zygo-proxy/src/provider.rs:19-25`); the launcher already keeps them from the agent (AU64). |
| One plugin sync per `up` (`skip_sync`) | The marketplace locks already serialize the per-launch syncs. |
| `-L <label>` and `default` server forms | A label resolves through `TMUX_TMPDIR`, which can differ between shells and sandboxes, so one workspace could look gone from one shell. One absolute path with `-S` cannot (AU62). |
| Move `results/` out at exit and back in at resume | Two moves and a signal window. A symlink to one per-member directory needs neither (AU16). |
| Save overrides and the compaction override only at `down` | A crash or reboot loses them, and `down` needs a status read. A write at each change keeps them with no read (OP12, AU105). |
| No slash commands for workspace verbs (earlier AU21) | Against O14 **(operator, OP11)**. |
| A `resume_failed` state and `--retry-failed` | A state that only blocks the next try. Every `up` tries again and prints the command (AU60). |
| Gate bypass flags on `up --all` | The flags are printed at every `up`; a gate adds a flag and a second path (AU70). |
| Drain idle rule from notifications and a quiet time | Premises PW12/PW13. The proxy request state gives the signal for model work (AU26, AU91). Tool events give one count only (`tools_running`, W10 rounds 2 and 3): a tool call sends no model request, so without the count a tool that runs while `busy` is `false` looked idle. Above 0 the member is busy whatever `busy` holds (never lose work). The cost is two `zygo hook` processes for each tool call of a member (`PreToolUse` and `PostToolUse`, `*` matcher); a launch that is not a member gets neither. |
| A `window-pane-changed` hook, to record an active pane closed while another session is current on tmux 3.7c and earlier | It fires at each `select-pane` (`fixtures/tmux/e1-hooks-<version>.txt` case G), so it is a focus hook (AU90): one snapshot and one state write for each focus change. Only shell panes are affected, and the next trigger or `down` writes them (W§7.4 known limit). |

| § | Change |
|---|---|
| §2 | Add goal G11 Workspaces (W-G1 to W-G6). |
| §3 | No change. Phase 2 non-goals are in W§3. |
| §4 C1 | Add: workspaces need tmux; native Windows has none (OP5). |
| §5 | Add the terms of W§5. "Session" stays. |
| §6.1 layer 2 | Cannot set `[loadout]`, `[workspace]` (AU22). |
| §6.1 layer 3 | Add `[workspace]`, `[loadout.<name>]` (W§11). |
| §6.1 layer 4, 5 | No change; the repository file keeps its four tables, and a profile merges only `features` (AU22). |
| §6.1 layer 7 | A member's session overrides survive `down`/`up` and crashes: saved at each change, applied at resume (OP12, AU105). |
| §7.1, §18.2 A2 | No daemon stays. A2 "session state dies with the session" gains: "except the workspace state, which outlives sessions" (AU1). |
| §7.2 | The session status reply gains `requests_in_flight` and `last_request_end` (AU91, OP13). |
| §8.1 | Add capabilities WA-1 Resume and WA-2 Turn signals (AU28); add the cells to `docs/conformance.md`; OpenCode cells partial until PW4 passes (AU104). |
| §8.2 step 1 | Membership check before the launch (W§8.4); `--session-id` pre-assign (W§8.3); resume argument (W§8.2). |
| §8.2 step 2 | The launcher calls the member callback after it creates the session directory and before the agent starts (AU34, AU92). The view gets the resume note from `LaunchOpts` (AU72). |
| §8.2 step 5 | For members, the exit is classified (AU17, AU73); an exit before the spawn keeps the member (AU60, AU92). |
| §8.2 step 6 | No change to the unit fold: units end at exit (AU31). A member's `results` is a symlink, so its session directory stays until the sweep of AU69 (AU16). |
| §8.4 Set | Add `ZYGO_WORKSPACE`, `ZYGO_PANE` for members; a non-member launch removes them (AU58). |
| §8.4 denylist | Add `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CLAUDE_CODE_BRIDGE_SESSION_ID` (AU64). |
| §8.5 hooks | Add the member hooks of W§9.1, `StopFailure` included (AU57). A member whose merged settings set `disableAllHooks: true`, or that is launched with `--bare` or with `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` on (W AU26), runs none of them (§8.5 Flags), and the drain counts it as busy (W AU26). |
| §8.5 Flags | A Claude Code start whose agent gets `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` on by Claude Code's rule (W AU26) is bare mode: the session log records it as degraded with the line `degraded: CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE`, as it records `degraded: --bare`. |
| §8.6 | Add the resume note (W§9.4) as item 8, after item 7: Claude Code prints it once, at the first main-loop `SessionStart`; OpenCode adds it to every main-role request while its inputs are in the session view (W§9.4, AU104; `crates/zygo-hooks/src/workspace.rs:611-628`). |
| §8.7 | For members, the plugin sends a `session` call at load (the commit), a `session` call for each new main session (the known top session activated last; kinds only from `info`), `prompt` and `stop` when its turn set becomes non-empty and empty, `pre-tool` with `tool_name` `Agent` for the `task` tool in every session (waited for; a deny throws), and `pre-tool` for every other tool of a session that is not a known child and `tool-end` after each counted call (the AU27 tool count) (W§9.1, AU27; `crates/zygo-launch/assets/opencode-plugin.js:152-178`). |
| §10.1 | Results of a member stay readable after a resume through the symlink (AU16). The sweep deletes ended session directories linked to a workspace (AU69) and skips `ws` verbs (AU71). |
| §10.2.4 | A member's compaction override survives `down`/`up` and crashes (OP12, AU32, AU105). |
| §12.3 | `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` on in the agent's environment (W AU26) → exit 2: "--passthrough does not work with CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE: bare mode reads only API keys", as `--bare` is refused. |
| §13.3 | No change; W§9.4 tells the agent that its units were closed (AU31). |
| §15.4 | Add `[features] workspace_tool`, default `false` (the one toggle that defaults off); a profile cannot set it (AU65). |
| §17 | Add the rows of W§12. |
| §17.1 | Add rows `down [--now]` (the workspace of this session; detached), `ls`, `workspace` (OP11). Session setting verbs and `compact` also write the member record (AU105). |
| §18 | Add OP1-OP13 and AU1-AU105 (withdrawn ids marked); add W§14 to §18.3. |
